**GR (grow-from-nothing redesign) specified — 2026-10-23; next: GR-01.** The user described seven long-standing problems (creatures too big with one per tank, automation and helpers too easy and too early, only two cultures, too few creatures and no feel of discovery, corridor-like rooms, stations that start maxed) and accepted all twenty recommendations based on market research (*A Game About Digging a Hole*, leaf and needle games, *TCG Card Shop Simulator*, *Megaquarium*, *Fish Tycoon*, *Palworld*, *Little Alchemy*). **GR.1–GR.18 at the end of this file** is now the authoritative design for the core loop: five eras from a back room to the Grand Exhibition; tanks measured in spaces (4 to start) with small, medium and large creatures; seven cultures; never-fail mixing for founders and Palworld-style breeding for hybrids (about 36 species, 144 entries); automation built up through power and pipes (T0–T4); helpers earned by stars plus an achievement; every station with 3–5 levels; a real square shop and back-room lab that grow by opening walls. It replaces AD-F and absorbs AD-E. Next: **GR-01** (roster, tables and a pacing simulator, reviewed by the user), then the GR-02 greybox and its playtest. AD-E's playtest and AD-D's look are still open and feed GR-02.

## 1. Read this first

This is the single current guide for project rules, product direction, milestones, task status, validation summaries, and continuation. On this Windows workspace, `Agents.md` resolves to this existing `AGENTS.md`; do not create a competing file with different capitalization. Keep the canonical uppercase filename for agent discovery.

The user approved the front-store/back-lab direction and requested this full-game plan. The user has now requested implementation of the next ready assignment. Work through the bounded assignments below; this is not authorization to implement the entire roadmap in one turn. Future implementation requests select a bounded milestone. A request to continue can start the next ready milestone without repeated permission questions about routine decisions.

This guide supersedes the original brief's single-room layout, its old M02/M03 roadmap, and the old requirement to maintain several parallel planning files. The original brief (`Evolution_Shop_Prototype_Handoff.md`), the old game's planning files (`TASKS.md`, `RESUME.md`, `UnityProjectContext.md`, `VALIDATION.md`, `PROTOTYPE_BRIEF.md`, `Design/`) and its records from this guide (`old-game-AGENTS.md`) are kept in `Docs/Archive/` as history since GR-17 (2026-10-28), when the old (classic) game was removed from the project; their scope and status statements do not override this plan. Store raw logs, reports, and captures under `Docs/Evidence/`, and summarize new work here. Do not create another live roadmap or task dashboard.

User instructions take precedence over this guide. Separate **user requirements**, **recommended design defaults**, and **verified implementation**. A plan, generated source, or attractive screenshot is not proof of a working feature.

## 2. Project identity and protected boundaries

| Item | Current verified baseline |
|---|---|
| Approved workspace / Git root | `C:\dev\DumbVideoGameStudio` |
| Unity project | `C:\dev\DumbVideoGameStudio\EvolutionShop` |
| Editor pin | Unity `6000.3.21f1` (`c02631ffc030`), explicitly authorized patch |
| Packages | URP `17.3.0`; Input System `1.19.0`; Test Framework `1.6.0` |
| Editor executable | `C:\Program Files\Unity\Hub\Editor\6000.3.21f1\Editor\Unity.exe` |
| Playable scene | `EvolutionShop/Assets/EvolutionShop/Scenes/Shop.unity` |
| Current validation player | `Builds/Windows-6000.3.21f1-gr17-one-game-2/EvolutionShop.exe` (GR-17, numbered run 2; `Play-GR16-look.cmd`, `Play-GR12-creatures.cmd` and `Play-GR11-exhibition.cmd` beside it open the development start points); the run script is `Docs/Evidence/gr-17/Invoke-GR17.ps1` |
| Local save | `%USERPROFILE%\AppData\LocalLow\DumbVideoGameStudio\EvolutionShopPrototype\growth-gr02.json` (the game's own save line, GR.15); the removed classic game's `evolution-shop.json` there is left as it was, never read or written |
| Scene generation | `EvolutionShop.Editor.ProjectSetup.CreateScene`; menu **Evolution Shop > Rebuild playable scene** |

- This is an independent game. Never modify or import files, code, assets, history, or settings from Dungeon Project, Dungeon Roguelite, or PersonalAssistant.
- Work within the approved workspace. Preserve user work and saves. Do not reset history, delete unrelated files, change global settings, or publish remotely.
- Use ordinary workspace permissions. If the tool sandbox requires escalation, request only the specific authorized path/action. Never seek unrestricted access or work around a denied operation.
- Unity execution note (verified 2026-10-11): a restricted CLI process may refuse LicenseClient-retai IPC even when Hub licensing works. Preserve the failure and request scoped normal desktop execution for the exact pinned validation command before diagnosing a missing license. Filesystem write permission alone does not verify IPC access. Current successful commands/receipts are under Docs/Evidence/r08-4/retry-20261011-*; retain normal-save and existing-process protections.
- No asset purchase, paid generation, account/backend, engine upgrade, plugin installation, or external publication is implied by this roadmap.
- Before Unity work, inspect the installed executable, project version, modules, processes, and exact project path reported by the editor/connection. An old MCP status file is not connection evidence. Verify identity before editor mutations; retain the existing exact-project guard.
- Documentation-only work does not require launching Unity or claiming an active editor connection. Verify runtime identity again when implementation resumes.

## 3. Product contract

### User requirements

1. Separate physical rooms: a customer-facing front store and a production/research lab behind it.
2. A day/night rhythm: develop and grow creatures at night; personally work in the store during the day, especially at the beginning.
3. Store progression: expand, buy display tables/tanks, decorate, hire workers, attract more customers, and earn higher prices through a better business.
4. Lab progression: expand, improve equipment, unlock more culture/creature types and production processes, and ultimately automate routine work.
5. Deliberately silly interactions: cartoon customer slaps, funny noises, absurd spoken reactions, farts, and other short comic incidents.
6. A satisfying main ending. Collecting everything is a possibility, not an approved mandatory ending.

### Intended experience

Start as a hands-on creature grower and shopkeeper. Learn what customers want, discover strange aquatic creatures, improve the two rooms, and gradually become the designer of a functioning business. Automation should remove mastered repetition while leaving discovery, planning, presentation, pricing, and optional personal service worth doing.

The daily loop is **plan and grow at night → display and sell by day → review results → invest in store or lab → try something new**. Each cycle should produce a meaningful decision or a visible improvement, not just another round of compulsory refilling.

Moonlighter is the user's reference for the two-part shopkeeper rhythm. Its publisher describes a shopkeeper/adventurer duality; this game substitutes laboratory discovery for adventure. Do not import its characters, art, combat, or dungeon scope. Reference: [11 bit studios — Moonlighter](https://11bitstudios.com/games/moonlighter/), checked 2026-10-03. The concrete systems below are original recommendations for this project.

## 4. Recommended ending: the Grand Exhibition

**Design default, open to user revision:** earn the opportunity to host a Grand Exhibition in the expanded store. Prepare a curated living collection, show that the lab can reliably support the shop, and complete a special trading day. Finish with a short celebration, a visible shop award, and a results page; then continue the same save in free play.

This ties together discovery, store quality, service, staffing, and automation. A collection counter alone would reward only one part of the game and could make the final hours depend on repetitive or lucky discoveries.

Provisional implementation contract:

- The objective board shows progress from the first few days; the ending must not appear as a surprise late requirement.
- Unlock the event after reaching the final planned store and lab tiers, discovering 18 of the 24 species across all four families (AD.3; previously 9 of 12 across three) (including the three signature species) [**superseded 2026-10-23 by GR.12:** 48 of 72 species across all nine families and any 5 of the 9 crown species], and completing one ordinary shift with a working automated production-to-sale route. Identify those signature targets early on the objective board.
- Prepare three designated signature species, one from each family, in protected exhibition displays [**superseded 2026-10-24 by GR-11, the user's choice:** five different crowns on display in tanks on the hall's five spotlit places, protected while the exhibition is prepared and under way; the six purchases are announced when it is prepared]. Their acquisition is deterministic through research clues and known processes; they cannot be accidentally sold by a worker.
- Prepare for six clearly listed exhibition purchases using species already unlocked. These are preannounced ordinary customer purchases through the existing queue and checkout, not a new quest-delivery system. Targets and counts are tunable; the event introduces no new service mechanic that day.
- Complete all six purchases while keeping the three exhibition specimens on display. Failures affect only this attempt: retain upgrades, discoveries, remaining unsold stock, and legitimately earned money; release unpaid reservations. Completed sales stay committed: never restore sold specimens while retaining their payment. Show why the attempt failed and allow another preparation night. No campaign restart or irreversible failure.
- Completion is awarded once and survives save/load. Free play keeps the upgraded shop and lab. Completing all 96 journal entries (AD.3; 288 under GR.7), optional customer requests, alternate layouts, and efficiency challenges are extra goals, not prerequisites for the main ending.

Validate this goal with a rough, shortened playable version before building all final content. If business-oriented players find it too collection-heavy, reduce the discovery requirement; if collectors find business steps excessive, shorten those gates. Preserve a clear finish rather than expanding requirements indefinitely.

## 5. Bounded launch scope

The following numbers are **planning ceilings and tuning defaults**, not promises or measured balance. Change them with evidence and record the decision here before multiplying content.

| Area | Proposed first finished release |
|---|---|
| Platform | Offline, single-player Windows PC, keyboard/mouse; one shop and adjoining lab |
| Room growth | Three store tiers and three lab tiers, including the starting tier; modular extensions of the same location. **Superseded 2026-10-23 (GR.11):** a square 8 × 8 m shop and back room growing by opened walls to units A–C, a lab annex and a workshop |
| Discoveries | **Superseded 2026-10-19 (AD.3):** 24 species in four families with four deterministic colour variants each (96 journal entries), including the existing five; four culture types total. Earlier ceiling: 12 species in three families. **Superseded 2026-10-23 (GR.7):** 72 species in nine families (the user's choice at GR-01's review; first planned as 36 in six), 288 entries, seven cultures, mixing and breeding |
| Production | Culture growing, incubation, and one later conditioning process; a small set of upgrades and automation modules. **Superseded 2026-10-23 (GR.8–GR.9):** stations with 3–5 levels, a breeding tank, and automation built through power and pipes (T0–T4) |
| Shop furniture | Small/large display tables or tank stands, counter, storage; approximately eight reusable décor items. **Superseded 2026-10-23 (GR.5):** Starter, Large and Show tanks measured in spaces |
| Customers | Four behavior archetypes with shared navigation/transaction systems and varied presentation |
| Employees | Three roles: cashier, stocker, lab technician; at most one active employee per role initially. **GR.10 (2026-10-23):** each earned by stars plus an achievement, with levels and training |
| Humor | Six short reusable incident types with cooldowns and reaction variants |
| Campaign | One progression path and one Grand Exhibition ending; optional continuation afterward |
| Content standard | One approved visual/audio sample before bulk production; readable, coherent assets rather than many rough variants |

Out of scope unless the user expands the contract: multiplayer, combat/dungeons, another explorable town, several shops, land-animal systems, realistic water chemistry, creature death/feeding chores, complex genetics, breeding pedigrees, random shiny hunting, conveyor physics (GR, 2026-10-23: two-parent breeding with deterministic results and logical pipes without fluid physics are in scope; pedigrees, genetics simulation and physics stay out), loans/debt simulation, offline income, real-time waiting, subscriptions, cloud accounts, and paid acceleration. Decorations and lab machines must use the same small placement system rather than separate editors.

## 6. System specifications and design rules

### 6.1 Day and night

- Begin a new game with a guided preparation night. Use the existing simple recipe to prepare the first shop stock, then let the player open the store.
- Model explicit phases: **Night preparation → Open day → Closing → Night preparation**. Store day index, phase, clock, transition state, and any once-per-day payments/rewards. Transition commands must be safe under rapid input and reload.
- *(GR, 2026-10-23: 5:30 days from AD-E pending its measured playtest; GR.3.)* Start testing with an 8-minute trading day and an untimed preparation night. Allow the player to finish preparation when ready. These are hypotheses; measure workload and waiting before choosing final timings.
- The simulation advances during active gameplay and ordinary station panels. Pause/settings stop it. Closed application time does not advance it. Night is a production/planning space, not mandatory idle waiting.
- Growers and incubation run during both active phases. Daytime lab access is allowed: the cost is attention to customers. Staff later make that tradeoff easier. Do not arbitrarily lock the lab door.
- Closing stops new arrivals. Complete existing accepted transactions or cancel them safely after a clearly signaled grace period; release reservations and never pay for an unfinished sale. No customer pathfinding error may prevent the next night forever.
- Provide open/close confirmation when customers or accepted orders would be affected. Show a daily report with sales, stockouts, lost customers, wages, and the next improvement opportunity.
- Construction, hiring, and major rearrangement happen at night. Repricing future unreserved stock and ordinary restocking can happen during the day.

### 6.2 Hands-on shopkeeping and customers

- The early day must require the player: open the shop, choose stock/prices, serve the counter, and replenish displays. Merely pressing a remote sell button is not the finished store loop.
- Customer states: arrive, browse a reachable display, decide, reserve a specimen, queue, transact, react, leave. Start with one customer and a bounded queue, then increase the configured concurrency.
- One specimen has one owner and one reservation. A sale atomically removes that exact specimen and pays once. Customer cancellation, impatience, closing, or a gag releases the reservation without deleting stock.
- Show readable price reactions and reasons for a lost sale. Later archetypes differ in preferences, budgets, patience, and reactions: bargain shopper, casual visitor, collector, and enthusiastic premium buyer.
- The player's price is bounded and validated. Lock the agreed price when a customer commits; repricing a display must not change a queued sale. Queue length, walking space, and service time limit practical demand.
- A developed shop attracts a larger bounded arrival rate and unlocks customers with larger budgets. Presentation and reputation raise willingness to pay within caps; customers still value particular creatures and fair prices.
- Keep the stationary prototype buyer until the first real customer loop works, then retire that shortcut from normal play. Keep development shortcuts separate and clearly labeled.

### 6.3 Economy and progression

- Separate cash, business reputation, display appeal, and discovery progress. Give each a visible purpose; do not conceal all progress behind an unexplained score.
- Prefer capped additive modifiers for presentation, service, and species preference. Avoid multiplying every store upgrade into both unlimited traffic and unlimited price. Repeated copies of the same decoration have diminishing or no additional economic benefit.
- More stock capacity, faster handling, new customer demand, and new recipes should create distinct choices. Preview costs and concrete benefits before purchase; avoid upgrades that are only imperceptible percentages.
- Keep starter cultures renewable for free and at least one profitable recipe available at every stage. Zero money, a full incubator, an unstaffed shift, or a failed exhibition must remain recoverable.
- Introduce wages only once ordinary income is reliable. Default: schedule staff at night; a paid duty period covers that preparation night and the following trading day. Charge once before an employee first starts work in that period, so nighttime lab work is covered. Persist employee ID, duty-period ID, and payment; dismissal/reactivation cannot avoid or duplicate the charge. Preview that started periods are paid in full. Insufficient funds leave the employee off duty with a clear message; the player can always work manually. No hidden debt or permanent staff loss.
- Centralize balancing in definitions/configuration. Record income per shift, operating cost, stockouts, waiting time, and days to upgrades using local test data. Do not add external analytics without authorization.
- Keep a balance worksheet/table inside this file when needed. Do not commit to campaign hours, prices, hardware targets, or production dates without measurement.

### 6.4 Store/lab layout, construction, and decoration

- Preserve the front entrance and recognizable passage to the back lab. Expand the store sideways; extend the lab behind it. Prioritize a clear path from entry to displays to counter to exit, and a short stock route from lab to store.
- Use snapped footprints and explicit access points. Validate room bounds, non-overlap, doorway clearance, reachable service positions, queue space, and navigation before committing a placement or charging currency.
- Keep at least a provisional 1.2 m clear main aisle; widen after crowd tests. Exact requirements come from player/agent dimensions and measured congestion, not decorative grid appearance alone.
- Provide a ghost preview, rotate, cancel, move, and a recoverable storage option. Distinguish a visual display table from the stock capacity it provides; décor must not silently grant fish storage.
- Block movement/removal of a stocked or active machine until contents are moved safely, or perform a validated relocation transaction. Never destroy creatures, pending production, jobs, or paid furniture through rearrangement.
- Build navigation around actual doors, station access, queues, and worker destinations. After layout changes, rebuild/update only the necessary navigation and recheck existing jobs. Unreachable placements should be rejected rather than trapping actors.
- Keep storefront and laboratory visually distinct through materials, lighting, signs, sound, and props. Validate world-label occlusion, silhouettes, sightlines, and HUD coverage from normal player height.

### 6.5 Research and laboratory growth

- *(Superseded 2026-10-23 by GR.7: never-fail mixing resolves any mix to the nearest founder, and breeding makes hybrids; known-recipe selection stays.)* Preserve the proven exact-integer recipe model and convenient known-recipe selection. First discoveries should give a visible new silhouette/behavior, a journal entry, and a useful production or market choice.
- Add culture types and one new processing method gradually. Use purchasable research/facility unlocks and journal clues so experimentation is directed. No required discovery depends on an unbounded random roll.
- Higher recipes may require a processing stage as well as culture amounts. Keep the UI clear about ingredients, facility, time, destination, and expected result for known recipes. Do not turn this into a genetic simulation.
- Separate unique specimen identity from species definition. Model stock locations such as incubator output, lab storage, display, customer reservation, and sold. Movement changes ownership once; presentation objects are not authoritative inventory.
- Machines visibly expose inputs, work in progress, output, capacity, and blocked reason. Reserve output space before consuming ingredients, or safely retain the completed output in the machine. Full storage pauses the chain.
- Add comfortable shortcuts for mastered work: batch orders, known recipes, target stock levels, and reusable machine settings. Preserve manual operation and cancellation/recovery throughout progression.

### 6.6 Staff and automation

- *(GR.8 and GR.10, 2026-10-23: automation is built through power and pipes, and helpers are earned by stars plus an achievement.)* Automate chores only after the manual version is understandable and useful. Give the first helper one clear job before building a general assignment system.
- Cashier: serve valid queued transactions. Stocker: move explicitly available stock to configured displays. Lab technician: perform assigned known production work. Machines provide predictable processing; staff handle interaction/transport and service.
- Every job has a stable ID, one owner, reserved resources/destination, a state, and an understandable blocked reason. Assign priorities and allow pause/reassignment. Avoid two actors claiming the same specimen, slot, or sale.
- On dismissal, path failure, room editing, cancellation, or load, finish or safely release ownership/reservations. Retry with a bounded policy and visible status; never silently discard a job or retry forever while consuming resources.
- Default control: desired stock count per known species, destination display/storage, enabled facilities, worker role/priority. Show shortages and why a route is idle. Avoid an abstract node-graph editor for the first release.
- **End goal of automation:** a known recipe can replenish from renewable growers through processing, storage, display, and customer sale across a complete shift without repeated collection/dosing/restocking/checkout clicks. Full buffers halt safely; supply or space restoration resumes work; save/load preserves the result.
- Full routine automation is optional to operate day-to-day and a demonstrated capability for the proposed ending. The player still chooses discoveries, product mix, prices, expansions, display design, exhibition stock, and optional special requests. Never sabotage working automation with compulsory breakdown chores just to force activity.

### 6.7 Comedy and personality

Use brief authored setups, clear reactions, and fast recovery. Suggested six incident types:

| Incident | Player/character beat | Mechanical limit |
|---|---|---|
| Cartoon slap | Deliberate separate action; exaggerated face, wobble, silly sound, grumbled gibberish | Ordinary shopper may cancel and leave; clownish visitor can laugh. Small capped reputation consequence; release stock reservations. No payment reward for repeated slaps. |
| Suspicious fart | Brief squeak, delayed reactions, a customer blaming the display fish | Cooldown and audio variation; never removes valuable stock or blocks a doorway. |
| Ridiculous complaint | “This fish looks like my accountant” and an expressive reaction | Short optional dialogue; standard skip; no mandatory voice performance budget. |
| Incubator belch | A successful specimen reveal ends with a silly burp or bubble | Never signals failure or masks the completion cue. |
| Overenthusiastic worker | Tiny victory dance or squeaky shoe after completing a job | Only when idle/safe; cannot abandon a customer or freeze the task queue. |
| Fish copies a customer | Creature imitates a pose or makes an absurd noise | Visual/audio reaction only; fish stays inside bounds. |

- Do not bind a slap to the ordinary service/interact button. Add remappable intentional input and a clear target prompt. Never strike on opening a menu.
- Gags share cooldown/concurrency limits so the room is not constantly shouting. Use readable captions for important reactions and separate voice/humor volume plus a reduced-gags option.
- Default to cartoon slapstick, invented mild swearing/gibberish, and no gore. Profanity intensity, final dialogue style, and intended age rating remain user-facing content decisions before recording final assets.
- Gags must tolerate the target leaving, closing time, pause, save/load, and interrupted animations. They must not corrupt a sale, trap an NPC, punish a player repeatedly, or become a superior money/reputation farm.

## 7. Technical implementation rules

Current code under `EvolutionShop/Assets/EvolutionShop/`: `Core/GameTypes.cs` and `GameModel.cs` own rules/state; `Runtime/ShopSession.cs` coordinates play; `ShopHud.cs` presents UI; `ShopPlayer.cs` handles movement/input; `SaveStore.cs` handles saves; `Presentation/ShopWorld.cs` builds the procedural space; `CreatureVisual.cs` renders creatures; `Editor/ProjectSetup.cs` generates the scene and builds; `Tests/EditMode/GameModelTests.cs` and `Runtime/MilestoneSmoke.cs` hold existing validation.

- Extend these boundaries incrementally. Extract clock, transactions, placement, and jobs when a milestone needs them; do not keep growing one session/model into every system, and do not build a speculative engine/framework first.
- Keep economic/production rules testable without scene objects. UI, animation, audio, and AI request validated commands; they must not independently alter money or inventory.
- Use stable IDs and versioned save DTOs. Definitions contain tuning and presentation references, not mutable runtime ownership. Avoid saving scene hierarchy paths or localized display strings as identity.
- Prefer deterministic rules and seeded test scenarios. Validate negative/overflow quantities, missing IDs, capacities, duplicate commands, interrupted work, and transaction order.
- Navigation and presentation may fail without losing economic state. Recovery must resolve a blocked actor/job and preserve valid inventory rather than teleporting unpaid stock into a sale.
- Evolve the generated scene deliberately. Current CreateScene replaces the prototype scene with its bootstrap; inspect ownership/unsaved edits before using it after authored scene work begins. Move reusable room/modules to prefabs when needed, while keeping a repeatable setup route.
- Preserve URP shader inclusion and depth-tested world text in standalone builds. Keep audio feedback distinguishable from comedy. No new third-party dependencies just to solve a small local problem.
- Record provenance/license and local asset source for every admitted asset. Approve scale, pivot, colliders, material/shader, animation, LOD/readability, and build inclusion before replacing placeholders. Paid assets/generation require separate authorization.

### R06.2 asset conventions — contract for the next assets

These are project decisions grounded in the R06.1 sample, not industry standards or human creative approval. Follow the current reference; obtain R06.5 tone feedback before multiplying it. The measurable subset runs in `Runtime/PresentationContractSmoke.cs` as part of `-evolutionPresentationSmoke`. It writes `asset-contracts.json` and five original PCM WAV references into the isolated evidence directory. It never admits assets, modifies the normal save, or changes audio gain.

**Scale, axes and identity.** One Unity unit is one metre; Y is up. Furniture/station roots sit at floor centre, unit scale, with the operator-facing front on local -Z. Characters have a floor/feet root facing +Z; creatures have a body-centre root facing -Z. Bake source object transforms; use a named visual child to adapt imported axes/scale once, retaining the existing station/actor root and stable ID. No negative root scale, mirrored normals, or animated scale on colliders. The presentation-only Pondlet turntable uses 1.5× scale; that is not the aquarium import scale. Preserve `.meta` GUIDs and use existing definition/specimen/facility IDs, never mesh names or hierarchy paths, as save identity.

| Reference | Root-space limits / intent |
|---|---|
| Casual visitor / employee sample | Mesh envelope X ±0.85 m, Y -0.04..1.85 m, Z -0.65..0.90 m across idle/walk/greet/carry. Measured top is approximately 1.746 m; the existing walk allows about 3.9 cm sole penetration, so the envelope includes a 4 cm floor tolerance. This is visual clearance, not a physical body collider |
| Pondlet | Mesh envelope X ±0.60 m, Y ±0.40 m, Z -0.50..0.85 m across fin/tail motion at root scale 1. Body is 0.50×0.43×0.72 m; whole-fish envelope includes fins/tail. Container-specific scaling and the existing tank-bound check remain authoritative |
| Bloom sample | Width 1.75 m × depth1.60 m, mesh Y0..2.50 m; measured mesh approximately 1.60×2.2525×1.435 m. Text and lights are reviewed separately from solid mesh bounds |
| Fixed first display | Preserve its existing station, tank interior, interaction collider and sample backdrop. The 2.40 m-wide wall panel at local Z1.17 is a fixed wall treatment, not an admitted movable-furniture footprint |

**Placement and collision.** `Core/ShopLayout.cs` owns 0.5 m grid offsets, 90° turns, footprints, service points and protected aisles. Original lab facilities retain their existing off-grid anchors. Visual size never silently expands the model footprint. Current local footprints (width×depth, metres): work table/small display/specimen rack 1.5×1; large display 2.5×1; conditioner 2×1; décor 1×1; Bloom/Flow 1.75×1.6; incubator 2×1.5; upgrade bench 1.75×1.25. Read definition functions when changing these; do not duplicate a layout registry in assets.

- Lab operator point: local (0,0,-1.5); rack/conditioner service point (0,0,-1.4); movable display browse points (±0.45,0,-depth/2-0.9), rotated with placement. Fixed displays/counter retain their existing access points.
- Keep the 1.2 m central aisle (X±0.6, Z-5.2..2.4), doorway clearance (X±1.6, Z0.05..0.95), and extension access protected. Existing reachability inflates obstacles by 0.35 m and samples a 0.25 m grid; runtime player/collider and customer NavMesh preflights must still pass.
- Decorative mesh children, fish, preview parcels and character rigs own no collider, trigger, Station, stock, or payment. Keep simple collision/interaction on the established furniture root. Current customers use NavMesh radius 0.28 / height 1.8 and pass through the player; the player controller is radius 0.3 / height 1.75. Do not turn costume detail or waving hands into navigation blockers.
- Validate every supported orientation and the nearest legal neighbour, operator/queue access, occupied relocation and save/reload when admitting a replacement. Bounds alone do not establish reachable interaction or readable labels.

**Silhouettes, palette and materials.** Read identity at normal play distance before adding small detail. Store: honey wood and teal cabinet shapes; lab: upright vessel, visible outlet and coral cap; Pondlet: rounded body, petal crest/heart tail and forward eyes; casual: cardigan/beret; employee: apron/work cap. Retain the existing long Ribbonfin, round Bubblebelly, lantern-bearing Lanternfin and winged Driftwing distinctions. Species, machine state and customer reactions need shape/text cues as well as colour. No roster or outfit multiplication in this task.

- Reference colours are the code-authored teal, cream, coral and honey-wood materials in `ShopWorldSample`, `PondletSample` and `ShopCharacterVisual`. Use URP/Lit for opaque parts; preserve current glass, emission and depth-tested `EvolutionWorldText` paths. Do not replace URP shader inclusion or accept a pink/missing-material fallback in a Windows build.
- Reuse materials within an asset; keep shader keywords and material ownership explicit. Procedural owners destroy their private materials/meshes. Imported shared assets are never destroyed as instance-owned resources. Glass remains limited to vessels/displays; use solid fin geometry, not alpha layers for every body part.
- Current samples have no textures or LODs. For a later approved imported replacement, start with one shared opaque palette material and at most one additional functional glass/emissive material where needed; document exceptions. Keep albedo readable without lighting baked into it; normals/non-colour maps use the appropriate linear import. Texture size and LOD transitions need measured screen coverage and profiling before a production budget is accepted.
- The procedural reference is not a performance target: sampled counts are Pondlet 20 mesh instances/13,856 triangles/5 material instances; casual 42/25,416/7; employee 41/23,892/7; Bloom 67/29,796/8. Counts include hidden parcel geometry and omit world text. They are neither draw-call counts nor frame-time evidence. Prefer consolidated meshes when producing approved replacements; compare actual runtime cost and silhouette before changing LODs. No performance claim or bulk reuse approval follows from these counts.

**Animation and event ownership.** Poses are in place. `CustomerActor`/navigation own visitor root movement; `ShopCharacterVisual` owns joints; `CreatureVisual` owns fish fins/tail; container presentation owns fish placement. Employee carry is an empty visual parcel, not an item or working employee. Preserve idle/walk/greet/carry and interruption back to idle. Presentation preview is paused simulation with explicit freeze/rotate/pose controls.

Future animation events may request presentation cues only (footstep, cloth rustle, hand contact, gag accent). Name the cue and its owning playback component; pass a validated cue ID, not an arbitrary method/string invocation. The owning action decides once whether a cue is allowed, with cooldown/re-entry protection. No clip event may transfer inventory, pay currency, finish incubation, charge wages, save, or commit a sale. Gameplay success is committed before feedback; missing/interrupted animation or audio must not undo or duplicate it. On pause, cancellation, closing, reload and disable, suppress stale events and reset transient poses. There are no Animator clips/events or employee jobs to wire in R06.2; real interruption/event scenarios remain R06.4/R07 work.

**Readability and review.** Inspect the real game at 1280×720 and a larger window, from the player eye (~1.6 m) and actual operator/browse/queue positions, in both night and day lighting. Preserve focus prompts, prices, reservations, progress and feedback text in front of trim, depth-tested against walls. Do not make ornamental lettering the sole way to discover a command. In the sample viewer, keep the subject between the existing header and footer; rotate it through 180° and check all four poses, then leave/re-enter and resume play. Creature admission includes the existing animated aquarium-bound sweep. Hand-inspection of new captures and a human legibility/tone pass remain necessary; the code audit cannot judge beauty, contrast, or comic timing. No global fix is claimed for historical long-panel footer scrolling.

**Audio conventions.** Keep gameplay cues short and recognisable; comic sounds must not mask transaction/blocked/discovery feedback. Original procedural cues are mono22,050Hz with no imported recordings, voice, music or AudioMixer. R06.3 uses two reusable 2D AudioSources at gain1: service and comedy. Saved master defaults to0.65; comedy/slap additionally uses finite, clamped Humor gain. Service replaces its current cue, stops comedy and owns a short protection window (0.25s; discovery0.8s). Comedy cannot stack or mask protected service. Missing/muted audio is never gameplay authority. Panel changes cancel incidental comedy, while a service tail may finish; Pause is not a master mute. See the R06.3 delivery contract for exact ownership and validation.

For the current short-cue family, use these **provisional authoring ceilings** before gain: sample peak≤0.25 (about -12.04 dBFS), full-clip RMS≤0.12 (about -18.42 dBFS), |DC mean|≤0.005, |first/last sample|≤0.005, duration≤1 second, finite non-silent PCM. Preserve the existing 22,050 Hz mono synthesis; do not resample merely to match another asset. Future recorded assets should retain a lossless source, license and explicit channel/rate/import settings; spatial diegetic audio requires distance/occlusion listening, not conversion of every cue to 3D. A longer approved gag requires its own envelope/tail measurement and a documented duration exception. These figures are ceilings, not a normalization target; never boost the quiet discovery cue solely to reach them.

| Original cue (`ShopAudio.cs`) | Frames / rate | Sample peak dBFS | Full-clip RMS dBFS |
|---|---|---|---|
| Siphon droplet | 2645 /22050 | -16.41 | -22.46 |
| Glass chime | 4410 /22050 | -17.62 | -23.74 |
| Soft blocked | 3087 /22050 | -16.72 | -22.79 |
| New life | 15434 /22050 | -23.49 | -30.48 |
| Soft pap and rubber boing | 6174 /22050 | -20.78 | -27.91 |

Historical R06.2 measurements summed one instance of each of the original five cues (0.586820 before gain). R06.3 now bounds playback to one service plus one comedy source and audits fifteen clips; the conservative maximum pair peak is approximately0.309918 before gain. This is not a resampling true-peak, integrated LUFS or device-output measurement. R06.5 still requires listening at default/full/muted master and Humor volumes, rapid valid/refused interactions and simultaneous service/gag requests. Captured WAVs are references; exported PCM cannot close that gate.

**Admission record.** Current five-view sample is source-authored procedural geometry using Unity built-in primitives plus the original solid Pondlet fin mesh; audio is original mathematical synthesis. Local sources: `Presentation/ShopWorldSample.cs`, `Presentation/PondletSample.cs`, `Presentation/ShopCharacterVisual.cs`, `Runtime/ShopAudio.cs`; `.meta` files are tracked. No external asset license, paid service, downloaded recording or other project source was introduced. For each future replacement, record in this guide: stable asset ID; source author/path and license/permission proof; import settings; scale/front/pivot/footprint; materials/shaders; pose/event ownership; measured bounds and resource counts; exact build/captures/audio evidence; human acceptance status. Keep vendor/source notices beside imported source files. A provenance record plus technical passes does not replace the outstanding creative gate.

### Save and migration contract

Persist day/phase/time, currency, cultures, discoveries, specimens and locations, machine progress/output, room tiers/layout, prices, staff/paid-duty-period state, job ownership, progression/exhibition flags, and settings. Transient customer visuals can be reconstructed, but accepted transactions and reservations need an explicit deterministic restore/cancel policy with no duplication.

Keep atomic writes and recovery backups; validate before replacing a known-good slot. Do not overwrite malformed or unsupported saves silently. Back up before schema migration and test representative prior saves, including the current Milestone 01 format. Never use the user's normal slot for automated tests. Retain local-only storage and no offline progression unless the user changes that direction.

## 8. How to execute work

### Model routing — user instruction, 2026-10-12

- Create GPT-6.1 Sol (`gpt-6.1-sol`) subagents for simple, bounded work such as inventories, mechanical documentation, and narrowly specified support tasks.
- Keep advanced design, runtime implementation, difficult debugging and technical review on GPT-6 Astra (`gpt-6-astra`; user calls this 6.0 Astra). Use an explicitly selected Astra subagent when the lead model cannot be changed or verified; do not claim to have changed the lead model.
- Record the actual model and file ownership for each delegated assignment. Give one writer per file and coordinate interfaces before parallel edits. Root owns integration, Unity process scheduling, commits and canonical AGENTS updates. No concurrent Editor/build processes. Simple agents must escalate ambiguous architecture or safety decisions to the advanced owner.
- This user instruction supersedes earlier root-only/no-separate-agents wording for future assignments. It does not authorize extra tasks beyond the selected milestone, external messages, engine/tool changes or paid services.


1. Read the current-state/next-action section and relevant contract above. Inspect Git status and affected code/evidence; preserve unrelated edits. Verify Unity identity when runtime/editor work is involved.
2. Select one ready milestone and one small task or tightly related group. State the intended playable result and acceptance check. Resolve routine implementation choices autonomously inside this scope.
3. Break a large task into smaller IDs before working. Give each active task an owner and a status. Parallelize independent reviews/assets/code only when file ownership and interfaces are clear.
4. Build the smallest complete playable slice. Use placeholders until behavior is tested; keep the existing loop functional. Fix regressions before expanding.
5. Run the strongest relevant checks that are actually available. Repeat or broaden tests when new changes, failures, or unresolved risks justify them; do not rerun everything solely to generate a larger count.
6. Review actual renders/audio/input for changed presentation. Separate automated execution, source review, screenshot review, and human playtest conclusions. Record failed attempts and fixes honestly.
7. After every completed assignment, update this AGENTS.md with status, actual evidence, limitations, and the next action before starting another assignment. Save a small local Git checkpoint after a coherent verified slice. Do not rewrite history or publish. Update this file's active task, evidence, known issues, decisions, and exact next action in the same checkpoint.
8. At a milestone gate, deliver the runnable build/scene, controls, change summary, executed test evidence, known issues, and the next playtest question. Obtain missing creative feedback before bulk content or irreversible production commitments; do not ask again for choices the user already made.

Task marks: `[ ]` planned, `[~]` active, `[!]` blocked with reason/next action, `[x]` acceptance checks executed successfully. Mark human acceptance separately. A checked implementation task is not a claim of enjoyment or creative approval. Use stable task IDs; record material scope changes rather than silently replacing requirements.

Before writing code, briefly consider all six perspectives: player decision/feedback, room navigation/readability, explicit system states/edge cases, maintainable implementation, falsifiable validation, and milestone dependencies/scope. Keep decisions concise and tied to the actual task.

### R10 — Beta: balance, usability, accessibility, performance

Depends on: R09 complete campaign. Output: a release candidate with measured limits and an empty critical-defect list.

- [ ] R10.1 Conduct fresh-player and experienced-player sessions; record onboarding failures, service load, income, upgrade pacing, night waiting, automation value, ending clarity, and gag repetition.
- [ ] R10.2 Tune progression/economy and trim chores. Verify finite caps, starter recovery, wage affordability, exhibition retry, and all required content paths.
- [ ] R10.3 Finish input rebinding, mouse sensitivity/invert, hold/toggle collection where appropriate, readable text/UI scaling, captions, non-color-only cues, audio channels, and reduced camera motion/gags.
- [ ] R10.4 Profile the densest supported layout, maximum customers/workers, occupied tanks, effects, and long-running simulation. Record frame-time percentiles, spikes, memory, and load times on named hardware/settings; optimize measured bottlenecks.
- [ ] R10.5 Exercise migration from prior supported saves, backup/corrupt-save behavior, alt-tab/focus, pause, resolutions/window modes, first launch, reset/cancel, quit/relaunch, and prolonged repeated shifts.
- [ ] R10.6 Close all release-blocking defects and document lesser limitations. Set supported hardware/settings from measurement; a fast development PC alone does not establish minimum requirements.

Gate: representative human playtests are recorded, no known progression/save-loss blocker remains, and the exact release candidate meets the agreed usability/performance targets. No feature expansion during stabilization.

### R11 — Final build and delivery

Depends on: R10 accepted release candidate. Output: a complete locally deliverable game, not an unfinished development scene.

- [ ] R11.1 Produce the Windows non-development release configuration; remove/disable smoke hooks, cheats, debug overlays, and F8 comparison in normal release play without removing diagnostics needed for support.
- [ ] R11.2 Run the exact packaged executable separately from editor tests: fresh start, normal save/load, a full representative shift, advanced automation, exhibition/ending, and settings/quit. Verify full campaign evidence still matches the frozen content.
- [ ] R11.3 Check credits, licenses/provenance, title/version, controls, settings persistence, clear save location, and known-issues/readme information. No unlicensed placeholder assets/audio may remain.
- [ ] R11.4 Package required runtime/data files, verify hashes against the tested build, exclude unwanted debug artifacts, and preserve the source tag and rollback build.
- [ ] R11.5 Record final test evidence, release-blocker count, accepted limitations, and human acceptance here; deliver the archive and concise play instructions.

Gate: the agreed game is playable from onboarding through ending and continuation, with tested saves and an exact verified release package. Uploading to a storefront, buying accounts/assets, marketing, and remote publication are separate user-authorized actions; a finished local build does not imply publication.

## 10. Validation standard and definition of done

| Layer | Required evidence when affected |
|---|---|
| Rules/state | Meaningful automated tests for transactions, timing/phases, recipes, capacity, pricing, reservations, wages, progression and job ownership |
| Persistence | Actual file round trips and migrations during production, phase transition, queue/worker handoff, room editing, and ending; isolated slots |
| Runtime integration | Real input, navigation, service/production routes, full/empty cases, pauses, interruptions, and recovery in a running scene |
| Presentation/feel | Actual captures plus hands-on input and listening; verify legibility, occlusion, crowding, audio overlap, and comedy timing |
| Standalone | Build result and a separate launch of that exact binary/package, with settings/hardware/context and observed results |
| Campaign | Supported fresh-start path to ending, optional continuation, zero-money recovery, advanced automation, and no inaccessible required unlock |

For each result record task ID, code checkpoint/build identity, command or manual steps, expected behavior, actual result, evidence path, and limits. Never say “tests/build passed” unless executed. Retain failed-run evidence; distinguish an automated pass from a screenshot defect or a human complaint. Test counts alone do not establish quality.

Classify defects by effect: **P0** save loss/security or destructive corruption; **P1** crash, impossible progression, duplicated money/stock, permanent actor/phase deadlock; **P2** significant usability/balance/presentation failure; **P3** minor polish. No known P0/P1 at a milestone handoff or release; explicitly report blockers instead of claiming completion. Release also requires unresolved P2 issues to be fixed or explicitly accepted with a practical limit. Avoid adding tests that merely mirror trivial code or documentation edits.

The game is finished when the launch-scope features, complete progression/ending, reliable automation, coherent art/audio, usable controls/settings, save migration/recovery, measured performance, and exact release package have passed their gates. A growing backlog does not postpone that definition indefinitely. Optional additions remain post-release ideas until authorized.

Waves 3 (the player's hands, yellow gloves) and 4 (§6.7's comedy) follow.
- **The people kit:** `Tools/Art/create_people_kit.py` (Blender 5.0, a few minutes; `--only Body_<kind>,Head,Held` rebuilds those and keeps the rest), which writes the record `ArtSource/PeopleKit/manifest.json` that `GrowthPeopleTests.TheKitsRecordMatchesItsFiles` checks. It executes the board's builder (`Tools/Art/gr18_characters.py`) as a library, with fewer segments, the materials as named slots `PK_<kind>_<colour or role>`, and parts grouped per joint and variant. A helper is a new `body_spec` kind and a kind in `people.json`.
- **The runtime:** `Runtime/Growth/GrowthPeople.cs` (the kit, looks, poses, motions and the person). `GrowthPersonPose.ToUnity` turns the board's Blender angles into Unity's (Blender (x, y, z) is Unity (-x, z, -y)); the motions are the board's formulas as code (not baked keyframes, as GR.55 first planned), a walk's phase advancing by the distance walked over its gait's `Cycle` (metres per two steps), so feet don't slide. `GrowthVisitorView.cs` shows a visit, and tells the routes what it is doing in `Activity` (walk, stand, browse, wait, pay, leave-happy, leave-shrug, leave-huff, leave-wave).
- **The people route:** `GrowthPeopleSmoke.cs` (`-evolutionGrowthPeopleSmoke`) puts visitors of its own into `shop.Visitors` (ids from 9001, browsing one tank until it sets their clock); unit A holds two ordinary visitors at once, so the rules' arrivals wait while there are two. The day's end opens the receipt, which pauses the shop's clock: the route closes it so the leavers walk out. In front of a browsing visitor is their tank, whose collider pushes the player's view away, so the route shows browsing from the side.

- **Commit without git's automatic maintenance:** `git -c maintenance.auto=false -c gc.auto=0 commit ...`. The repository's packs are about 23 GB; a plain commit started a repack that grew to 24 GB of memory and stalled run 2's player. A repack already running is stopped by ending its git chain; its leftover `.git/objects/pack/tmp_pack_*` (`git count-objects -v` reports it as garbage) can be deleted.
- **Rebuilding some kit pieces:** `create_growth_kit.py --only A,B` exports only those and keeps the other pieces' manifest records, so the other FBX files stay as they are. New builds go at the end of `BUILDS`, after wave 2's, so earlier pieces draw their random numbers as before.
- **Dressing a tank kind:** `DressTank` switches on the container's kind; the Starter tank's `TankFilter` is moved to any tank's back corner by its width, depth and height (`filterAt`); lids are per size (`TankLid`, `TankLidLarge`, `TankLidRack`).
- **Start points:** `GrowthStarts.Look` (`look`) is wave 2's; a new start point gets its own save prefix in `GrowthSession.Awake` and its launcher in `Invoke-GR16.ps1`'s build step.

Then, at their word, wave 2: the other stations and tanks (the vat, the breeding tank, the research bench, the supplier catalog, Large and Show tanks, racks), modelled from `gr16_look_ranch.py`'s station functions into the growth kit, a numbered run and the user's look; waves 3 to 5 as GR.45 has them. - **The kit:** `Tools/Art/create_growth_kit.py` (Blender 5.0, background, about 11 s) writes every piece to `Resources/Art/GrowthKit/` and `ArtSource/GrowthKit/manifest.json`. A piece is one joined mesh directly under its root; `place()` turns Unity's (x, y, z) into Blender's (-x, -z, y) and keeps meshes proper; materials are slots named GK_*, and every slot needs its entry in `LookSlots` (`GrowthWorldLook.cs`) and its colour in the manifest, or `GrowthLookTests` fails. A station's piece sits at its greybox root and inside its footprint (add it to the footprint TestCases); a room's dressing sits at the world's origin inside its room.
- **Placing:** `Kit(parent, asset, offset, cultureId, replaced...)` adds a piece as a decorative child, maps its slots, destroys any collider on it and switches off only the named greybox children's renderers (never their colliders or targets: AD.4). A level's parts go in `DressLevel` (from GR-06's `Look`), each with `SetActive` by level as the greybox's parts are.
- **The light:** `TickLook` blends four `LookKey`s (night, morning, midday, closing, from `GrowthPhase` and the day's clock) over 0.6 s. Realtime reflection probes are off in the project's quality settings for the classic game; the look switches them on at run time, and its probes render once the rooms stand and again after night and day change over (`Look / reflections ...`). A new dressed room adds its own probe.
- **The route:** `-evolutionGrowthLookSmoke` (`GrowthLookSmoke.cs`, captures 400 to 421); a wave adds its pieces' checks and captures there, and `Invoke-GR16.ps1` runs it at both sizes, with the density route again under `-evolutionGrowthPlain` for what the look costs.
- **Look renders:** `Tools/Art/gr16_look_ranch.py` (EEVEE, AgX Punchy, bloom in the compositor) is where a machine is drawn first for the user; the game's pieces follow its shapes and `create_growth_kit.py`'s palette, which matches it.

Fixtures, start points and older saves are Off; a route switches it on explicitly. The next-step line's words must fit one line (about 90 characters at most): the line is one line since GR-13 and ends in "…" past that.

A wrapping label in a row needs `flex-grow: 1; flex-basis: 0` to wrap at the row's width (`gr-check__text`).

- **Making a wave:** in `Tools/Art/create_diorama_kit.py`, replace the family's builders (same function names, so `SPECIES` and the atlas keep their order) with body-plan builders from its GR-12B section: `BPSpine`, `bp_profile` and `BPBody.at` (points on the surface), `bp_loft`, `bp_puffed`, `bp_plate` (soft fin membranes; `bp_outline` smooths an outline), `bp_strip` (long fin strips), `bp_fins` (mirrored fins), `bp_spots`, `bp_face` with `mouth_gape`, `mouth_grin` (along a snout), `mouth_arc` (a forward face) or `mouth_o`, and `bp_wave_keys` for an undulating body. Then add the species to `DioramaSpecies.Gaits` (`Presentation/DioramaCreatures.cs`) with its `DioramaGait`: that makes it remade (sized by span, swimming by its gait).

GR.34 foresees more for later families: fluke (a dolphin's, a whale's, a mosasaur-like tail beating up and down), paddle (an axolotl's, a frog's, a turtle's limbs), jet (a squid's dart), upright (a seahorse); add each as its family needs it, with its swim room in `GrowthCreatureView.Envelope` and its test in `RemadeSpeciesSwimByTheirBodyPlans`.
- **The rig still holds:** `DioramaCreatureTests.RigParts` (Body, Eyes, Pupils, Glints, Lids, Blush, Lips and the Tail, Left fin, Right fin and Crest pivots) on every model; an animal that doesn't blush keeps a hidden speck of Blush (`bp_face` without blush). A remade species' Tail pivot may sit anywhere behind its body's middle (a squid's cloak under it), and its lips 0.15 m ahead of its body's middle.
- **The four surviving breaks** (GR.35): the swim room's sampling of the crest's and keel's sway, the tail fin riding the wave, and a remade species' own stroke sizes change nothing for the Gloamer's eight; try them again with each wave, since a tall-finned sculler or a long-tailed undulator would need them.
- **The exhibition route's race** (GR-11, `GrowthExhibitionSmoke`): its second attempt keeps two of each announced creature in stock, but ordinary visitors can buy both between top-ups while its guest-watching stretch runs frame by frame; harden it with wave 2 (more in stock, or the announced creatures held back from ordinary visitors on the day), so wave 2's run covers it.

- **Adding a species** (each wave) is a builder in `Tools/Art/create_diorama_kit.py` on its family's rig (helpers: `nose_profile`, `on_body`, `on_ellipsoid`, `repaint`, `near`, `cone`, `taper_tube`, `side_fins`, `flame`, `gold_crown`, `lantern_on_stalk`, `loft_creature`, `round_creature`, `simple_part`), its four colours in `SPECIES` (append only: the atlas rows follow the order forever), a `Wave(...)` row in `DioramaSpecies.All` (`Presentation/DioramaCreatures.cs`: trick length, tempo, signature, crown), and its family's trick and habit in `DioramaTricks` (`Trick` by signature, `Idle` by family for species past AD-B's five, `Releases`). A new family also needs a `DioramaFamily` value, its water tint (`DioramaWater`), its strokes in `CreatureVisual.AnimateDioramaSpecies` (by family, at the species' `Tempo`), its burst in `GrowthCreatureBurst` and its family's placeholder retired (it goes by itself once `DioramaSpecies.TryGet` knows the species).
- **Building the kit:** Blender 5.0 (`C:/Program Files/Blender Foundation/Blender 5.0/blender.exe --background --factory-startup --python Tools/Art/create_diorama_kit.py -- --repo C:/dev/DumbVideoGameStudio`, about ten seconds). It rebuilds every model; the FBX bytes of an unchanged model differ (timestamps, ids) though its geometry is identical, so keep the committed bytes of unchanged models (check them by geometry first) and point the manifest's records at the files kept. The family review sheets are `ArtSource/DioramaKit/DioramaKit_<Family>_Front.png` and `_Side.png`. New FBX files get their import settings from `Editor/RedesignModelImporter.cs`.
- **In the greybox:** `GrowthCreatureView` builds a kit species with `CreatureVisual.BuildKit` (turned 180°, its body the size class's length, `CrownLength` 1.2 for crowns), its `Reach` and `HalfHeight` from every part (the tank's swim bounds use them), `Tick(t, reduced)` for its face, habit, strokes and trick (`TrickLinger` 0.8 s in peek, `TrickRest` 6 s), and `SetDetail` for its look (0 the full kit, 1 a baked mesh shared per species and colour with its outline, 2 without; `farLooks`); `GrowthWorld.Animate` picks the look per tank (the full kit within `KitDistance` 3.5 m and in view, outlined to `FullDistance` 6 m, plain beyond) and `TankView.ShowDetail` switches its creatures, and every tank places its creatures once even when frozen (`Placed`). `ColourOf` gives a kit species' atlas colour to cards and the journal.
- **The creatures start point** is `GrowthStarts.Creatures` (save `growth-gr12-creatures.json`); `GrowthStarts.KitFamilies` names the families done.
- **Routes:** `-evolutionGrowthCreaturesSmoke` (GR-12, `GrowthCreaturesSmoke.cs`: `Contain` measures every kit creature against its water every frame; `Linger` peeks at a creature until it plays its trick) beside GR-02 to GR-11's; `Docs/Evidence/gr-12/Invoke-GR12.ps1` runs them all (creatures at both sizes, the others at 1280×720) with AD-B's creature and AD-A's slice routes. The route harness now runs nested steps itself, so a check failing inside one stops the route with its reason.
- **Performance:** the density route's 180-creature stress across both units now averages 2.8 ms uncapped and GR-10's final layout (264 creatures) 4.0 ms (placeholders had 1.4 and 2.2 ms; full kit creatures everywhere 17.2 ms). A later wave with large species should check both again.
- **The classic game** shows only AD-B's five (`DioramaSpecies.Classic`: its creature study and AD-B's route); GR-12's species never enter the classic tanks.
