## GR — Grow-from-nothing redesign: start tiny, grow huge (2026-10-23)

Requested by the user on 2026-10-23, after AD-E was delivered. The user described seven long-standing problems, asked for recommendations grounded in market research and comparable games, and accepted all twenty ("Those look great"), asking for a detailed design spec here and for its tasks to be the next ones. This section is the authoritative design for the core loop, progression, creatures, production, automation, staff and spaces. Where it conflicts with §4–§6, AD.3 or AD.6, **GR wins** (the conflicting lines elsewhere are marked). Numbers are first-pass tuning defaults, to be set by GR-01's pacing model and changed only with playtest evidence.

### GR.1 — The problem, in the user's words, and what it means

| # | The user (2026-10-23, condensed) | Verified in the current game | Root cause |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Creatures are too big; they are fish, several should fit in a tank; bigger creatures should come with progress and need bigger tanks. | A fixed tank and a small display hold **one** creature each, a large display two; AD.4 made creatures about twice their old size. | No capacity axis; a tank is a single slot. |
| 2 | Automation should feel like *A Game About Digging a Hole* / the leaf and needle games: a weak tool, a clear goal, slowly building up to "electricity" that does the work; ours is too easy with all the helpers. | Collector (36) and doser (24) modules and three helpers are available almost at once. | Automation is a purchase, not a built-up capability. |
| 3 | Only two kinds of culture (the red and blue); the player should buy or research more. | Bloom and Flow; Spark and Mist only after research, and hard to notice. | Too few ingredients, hidden progression. |
| 4 | The creature selection is small and feels stuck; the player doesn't know how much to put in or what combinations exist; breeding games feel endless: put any two in, get something. | 5 species; recipes are exact integer counts; a mix that matches nothing uses nothing and gives nothing. | No discovery space and no feedback when experimenting. |
| 5 | The shop and lab feel like two small corridors, not a shop and a lab. | Store about 14 × 6 m, expanding sideways to 22 × 6 m; lab about 14 × 5 m, deepening by 4 m a tier. | Proportions and density, not just size. |
| 6 | Helpers come too early and the game turns to slop. | Hiring is free at the counter on the first night; wages 4 credits a duty period. | No gates. |
| 7 | Every station comes maxed out; no development; it feels empty. | Only upgrade: Siphon MK II (24 credits). | No station progression. |

**Diagnosis:** the game has no growth curve. Its references (*A Game About Digging a Hole*, leaf and needle games, *TCG Card Shop Simulator*, *Fish Tycoon*) start the player weak, give one clear goal, and make every upgrade visibly change how you play. GR rebuilds the game around that spine.

### GR.2 — Market research basis (2026-10-23)

- **Goal-and-upgrade games are short and live or die on pacing.** *A Game About Digging a Hole* (Feb 2025) reportedly reached over 250,000 players in its first week; dig, sell, upgrade (shovel to drill, inventory, battery, jetpack); 40 minutes to about 2 hours; its main criticism is being fully upgraded "before you've made much of a dent". Leaf games upgrade both tools and storage bins (*Leafscape*). Needle games change *how* you search per tier (hay bags, pitchfork, dynamite, vacuum; a plastic stick to radar). → Every upgrade must change the verb, not only a number; capacity is its own axis; protect the curve from being outrun.
- **Shop sims gate staff and space behind a shop level.** *TCG Card Shop Simulator*: experience from sales, workers from shop level 10 and more every 5 levels, expansions by level. *Supermarket Simulator* (per a player guide): cashiers at store levels 10/20/30/50 plus checkout counts. → Helpers and expansions are earned.
- **Aquarium games make size a spatial puzzle.** *Megaquarium*: bigger tanks hold bigger fish, social species need groups, tank capacity planned as count × size. *Fish Tycoon*: 21 bodies × 21 fins make over 400 fish, breeding is the only way to discover them all, the goal is 7 magic fish. → Capacity in spaces, size classes, and a breeding-driven collection with a clear finish.
- **Combination games feel endless when every pair yields something.** *Palworld*: hidden breeding values average to the nearest species, so any pair is predictable, with hand-authored special pairs. *Little Alchemy 2*: 4 elements grow to about 700 by pairs, no penalty for failure. *Potion Craft*: ingredients steer across a fogged map. *My Singing Monsters*' random chance and dead-end pairs are the model to avoid for required content.
- **Automation feels earned when power is a milestone** (*Factorio*'s burner-to-electric, *Satisfactory*'s early hand-fuelling); players split on how long the manual phase should last → keep it satisfying and short.
- **Competitors** (*Aquatic Store Simulator*, *Aquarist*, *Fishkeeper*, *Biotope*, upcoming breeding shop sims) are realistic fish-shop sims. **Evolution Shop's position:** invent whimsical creatures and grow from a back room to a showpiece aquarium shop; no competitor combines invention with the start-tiny curve.

Sources: Steam pages, reviews and community guides found 2026-10-23 (reported in the session; secondary sources, not official data).

### GR.3 — Pillars and targets

1. **Start tiny, grow huge, toward one goal.** The goal is the collection (discover every creature), and the Grand Exhibition is the finish line. The shop is the money engine that funds discovery.
2. **Every upgrade changes how you play.** An upgrade must add a verb, a capacity step the player feels, or a visual change; never an invisible percentage alone.
3. **Hands first, machines later, discovery always yours.** The player does each chore by hand until it is understood, then builds the machine that does it. Machines and helpers only ever repeat what is known; choosing new mixes and pairs is never automated.
4. **Readable abundance.** Tanks fill with small creatures; the shop looks alive and full.
5. **Never fail, never luck.** Every mix and every pair makes a creature. No required entry depends on a random roll (AD.3 rule kept). No death, feeding or breakdown chores (§5 kept).

| Target (first-pass, to be measured) | Value |
|---|---|
| First upgrade affordable | within 5 minutes |
| Something new (creature, unlock, upgrade) | every 10–15 minutes at most |
| First helper | no earlier than about 60 minutes |
| First powered automation | about 1:30–2:00 |
| Grand Exhibition available | about 3:30; main ending about 4:00 |
| Collect-all (288 entries; 144 when first planned) | 8–12 hours |
| Starting tank | 4 spaces |
| Day | 5:30 (AD-E prototype) pending the AD-E playtest's measured days; night untimed |

#### GR.3.1 — What stays and what goes

**Stays:** the day/night rhythm (§6.1); customers, queue, one reservation per specimen, the trade ledger and fair-price rules (§6.2); prices set by the player; AD-E's stars, wishes, themes, hands-on checkout and forecast; comedy and the slap (§6.7); the AD look, characters and creature rigs (scaled); the UI Toolkit interface (UX); staff job ownership, blocked reasons and recovery (§6.6); the placement system and its validation (§6.4); save safety (§7).

**Goes:** single-creature tanks and the fixed tank-a/tank-b; exact-recipe mixing that can make nothing; free hiring on the first night; stations that start maxed; the strip-shaped rooms and sideways-only expansion; automation bought as two modules.

### GR.4 — The progression spine (eras)

Five eras, each with a visible goal on the objective board (GR.12). Times are targets for a first-time player playing normally.

| Era | Time | Stars | Spaces | New verbs and systems | Cultures | Creatures | Helpers and machines | Era goal shown |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Back room | 0:00–0:30 | 1 | Shop unit A (8 × 8 m), back room (6 × 5 m) | Siphon by hand, mixing in the incubator, selling at the counter by hand, first station upgrades | Bloom, Flow | Brookkin founders (small) | None | "Discover 3 creatures and buy your second tank" |
| 2 Real shop | 0:30–1:15 | 2 | Unit B opens (shop 16 × 8 m) | Breeding tank, supplier catalog, Large tank, colour tints | + Ember (catalog) | First hybrids; Sparkline (small, with Spark research), Cinderling (medium) | Pip (cashier) from about 1:00 | "Breed your first hybrid; reach 3 stars" |
| 3 Plumbing | 1:15–2:15 | 3 | Lab annex (8 × 8 m) | Hand pump, pipes, generator (power), culture buffers | + Spark, Mist | Airfin (medium), Dewling (small) | Nori (stocker); powered pumps | "Power the lab; fill a Large tank" |
| 4 Machines | 2:15–3:15 | 4 | Unit C (shop 16 × 12 m), workshop | Auto-doser, incubator clutches, hatchling sorter, analyzer, Show tank | + Brine | Reefback (large), Saltscale (medium) | Moss (technician); hatchery line | "Run a full day on the line; discover a large creature" |
| 5 Exhibition | 3:15–4:00+ | 5 | Final tiers | Crown species, exhibition displays | + Dusk | Gloamer and Cometail (large), crowns | Full automation of known lines | "Host the Grand Exhibition" |
| Free play | after | 5 | — | Collect-all, all colours, optional challenges | all | all 72 species × 4 colours | — | "Journal: n / 288" |

Rules: an era's systems are introduced one at a time with a short contextual hint (R09.4); nothing from a later era is purchasable early, but its locked silhouette is visible (journal, catalog, station upgrade tabs) so the player sees what is coming. Money alone never skips an era: era gates are stars plus one concrete achievement.

#### GR.4.1 — The first ten minutes (beat sheet)

1. 0:00 Wake in the back room: two small growers (Bloom, Flow), a small incubator, a rack, a pinboard. One goal: "Make your first creature."
2. 0:30 Hold the siphon on a grower, dose 2 cultures, incubate (12 s). A Pondlet hatches with its NEW moment (AD-A). Carry it to the shop.
3. 1:30 The shop: an 8 × 8 m room, a front window, one Starter tank (4 spaces) and an empty stand, a counter by the door. Put the Pondlet in the tank.
4. 2:00 Make two more creatures; any mix gives something; one is a Bubblebelly (new), one a repeat Pondlet. The tank fills: three little fish swimming together.
5. 3:00 Open the shop. A Casual visitor with a wish bubble buys one. Hold to bag at the counter.
6. 5:00 First upgrade affordable: the siphon's bigger bulb (L2), or the grower's second shelf. The player chooses.
7. 7:00 Close; the receipt shows tomorrow's visitors and the next goal: the second tank.
8. 10:00 Second night: the journal shows Brookkin silhouettes with culture icons; the player tries a mix "close to something new" and finds the Ribbonfin.

### GR.5 — Creatures, sizes and tanks

**Size classes** (a species property):

| Class | Spaces | World body length | Families (GR.7) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Small | 1 | 6–9 cm | Brookkin, Sparkline |
| Medium | 2 | 12–18 cm | Airfin, Cinderling |
| Large | 4 | 25–40 cm | Reefback, Gloamer |

**Containers** (each a placed item; footprints on the 0.5 m grid):

| Container | Spaces (by level) | Footprint | Holds | Unlock |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Starter tank | 4 → 6 → 8 | 1.5 × 1 m | Small, Medium | Era 1 (two at start of play: one placed, one to buy) |
| Large tank | 8 → 12 → 16 | 2.5 × 1 m | Small–Large | Era 2, 2★ |
| Show tank | 16 → 24 | 3 × 1.5 m (wall or island) | All; lit, +appeal | Era 4, 4★ |
| Breeding tank | 2 parents (1 pair) → 2 pairs | 1.5 × 1 m, lab | Any class that fits (Large needs L2) | Era 2 |
| Lab rack | 4 → 8 → 12 spaces | 1.5 × 1 m, lab | Holding stock | Era 1 |

- A container holds any mix of species while their spaces fit. Each creature is still one specimen with one owner (§6.5 kept): containers hold an ordered list of specimens, not one slot.
- **Schools:** three or more of the same species in one container form a school: they swim together, and visitors accept 15 tolerance points more for that species there (capped, additive with themes, GR.10). Schools are the reason to keep several of one kind.
- **Comfort tags (Era 3+, optional):** each species has a temperament (calm or lively) and a water (fresh, brine or warm, set by a tank upgrade). A container whose occupants all agree gets +appeal (a "harmony" glow); disagreement only means no bonus. Never sickness, death or a sale blocked.
- **On-screen readability at the new scale:** looking at a container within 1.6 m eases the view into a **peek** (field of view 70° → 45° over 0.25 s; instant with Reduced motion; can be switched off). In peek, the creature under the reticle shows its name, colour and price, and can be picked (to move or to see a visitor's reservation). AD-B faces and glow carry over; small species gain a 1.3× head-to-body ratio for readability.
- **Swimming:** each container runs a bounded flocking swim (separation, alignment within a school, wall avoidance), with each species' AD-B idle habit and signature trick kept. Creatures never leave bounds (§6.7 kept).
- **Performance budget** (to be measured in GR-03): up to 180 creatures on screen at the final layout. Creatures beyond 6 m or out of view drop face blend shapes and use a cheaper swim; skinned meshes beyond 12 m freeze to a pose; per-container update spread across frames. Record frame times like AD-D.
- **Moving creatures:** the hand net (GR.9 siphon line) picks one creature in peek; a container card lists occupants as space icons; "Move all of a species" for mastered stock. Staff move by species targets as Nori does today.

### GR.6 — Cultures and the supplier

Seven cultures (names provisional; colours follow AD.4, only cultures and creatures fully saturated):

| Culture | Colour | Source | Unlock |
|---|---|---|---|
| Bloom | coral red | Grower (start) | Start |
| Flow | aqua blue | Grower (start) | Start |
| Ember | warm orange | Grower, from the supplier catalog | Era 2, 2★ |
| Spark | gold | Research bench (research) | Era 2–3, research points |
| Mist | opal lilac | Conditioning vat (research) | Era 3, research points |
| Brine | sea green | Grower, from the supplier catalog | Era 4, 4★ |
| Dusk | deep indigo | Research (analyzer) | Era 5 |

- Every culture is renewable once unlocked (§6.3 starter rule kept: Bloom and Flow are always free).
- The journal's **Cultures page** shows all seven from the start: owned ones with their source and level, locked ones as silhouettes with "Where to get it".
- **Supplier catalog** at the counter (a catalog book/phone station): orders placed at night arrive at the next opening. Sells containers, growers, decor and culture starter vials (a one-off vial lets the player try a culture before owning its grower). Catalog pages unlock by stars. No timers, no real-time waiting.

### GR.7 — Discovery: mixing and breeding

**Roster structure** (content made by family waves, GR-12): nine families × eight species = **72 species** (the user's choice at GR-01's review, 2026-10-23; first planned as six × six = 36), each with **4 colours** (AD.3: Classic, Sunny, Deep, Shimmer) = **288 journal entries**. Per family: 3 **founders** (made by mixing), 4 **hybrids** (made by breeding), 1 **crown** (a special pair); three small, three medium and three large families. The existing five keep their roles: Pondlet, Bubblebelly, Ribbonfin (Brookkin founders), Lanternfin (Sparkline founder), Driftwing (Airfin founder, now Medium).

| Family | Size | Lead culture | Era |
|---|---|---|---|
| Brookkin | Small | Bloom, Flow | 1 |
| Sparkline | Small | Spark | 2 |
| Cinderling (provisional) | Medium | Ember | 2 |
| Airfin | Medium | Mist | 3 |
| Dewling (provisional, new 2026-10-23) | Small | Mist | 3 |
| Reefback (provisional) | Large | Brine | 4 |
| Saltscale (provisional, new 2026-10-23) | Medium | Brine | 4 |
| Gloamer (provisional) | Large | Dusk | 5 |
| Cometail (provisional, new 2026-10-23) | Large | Spark, Dusk | 5 |

The model's family data must match this table (the Lanternfin is recorded as a Brookkin today; AD-E maps Spark species to Sparkline meanwhile).

#### GR.7.1 — Mixing (founders), never fails

- A mix is a multiset of culture doses. Its size is limited by the incubator level (L1: 2–3 doses, L2: up to 4, L3: up to 6).
- **Resolution:** an exact founder recipe makes that founder. Any other mix makes the founder whose recipe is **nearest** (sum of differences in each culture's count), among founders whose cultures the player owns; ties go to the lower era, then the journal order. So every mix makes a creature, and nearby mixes teach the space.
- **"Close to something new":** when a mix lands one dose from an undiscovered founder's recipe, the result card says so (always on). The microscope (research bench L2) shows how many doses away the nearest undiscovered founder is, a warmer/colder meter: the needle game's scanner.
- **Mix log:** every distinct mix tried and its result is kept in the journal; the incubator card offers known recipes in one click (§6.5 convenience kept).
- **Hints:** each undiscovered founder's silhouette shows which cultures it uses (icons, not counts) once the player owns those cultures.

#### GR.7.2 — Breeding (hybrids and crowns), any pair works

- The **breeding tank** (lab, Era 2) takes two creatures. After its timer (L1 60 s, L2 40 s, L3 two pairs) it produces one offspring (L3 clutch: two of the same) into its own output space; the parents stay (no consumption, no death).
- **Species result:** every species has a hidden **breeding value**. The offspring is the species whose value is nearest the parents' average, among non-crown species (*Palworld* model); a same-species pair breeds true. Hand-authored **special pairs** override the average and are the only way to the nine crowns. Every pair, including across families, yields something predictable. With 72 species there are 2,628 different pairs (2,556 of two different species).
- **Colour:** deterministic. Tinting the breeding water with a culture dose sets the offspring's colour (Bloom → Sunny, Flow → Deep, Mist → Shimmer); untinted offspring take the higher-order parent colour (Classic < Sunny < Deep < Shimmer). Founders hatch Classic unless the incubator is tinted (incubator L3).
- **Pair log and chart:** the journal records every pair tried; a family chart fills in as hybrids are found. The breeding planner (research bench L4) predicts the offspring of any two known species.
- **Breeding as production:** breeding two known creatures is a cheap way to make more stock without cultures, a real economic choice against mixing.
- **Research points (RP):** first discovery of a founder 5 RP, hybrid 8 RP, crown 20 RP, a new colour 2 RP. RP buy research (cultures, the breeding tank, analyzer levels, blueprints). Credits buy physical things. Sales never give RP; discoveries never give credits directly.

### GR.8 — Automation: building up to "electricity"

| Tier | Era | What the player builds | What it does | Power |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T0 Hands | 1 | Siphon, hand dosing, carrying, counter | Everything by hand | — |
| T1 Hand pump | 2–3 | A hand-cranked pump on a grower, a culture buffer tank by the incubator, pipe segments laid on the lab grid | Hold to crank: fills the buffer in bulk instead of per dose | Muscle |
| T2 Power | 3 | Generator (hand crank → bubble turbine → twin turbine → tide engine), electric pumps, the auto-doser | Growers feed buffers by themselves; the incubator doses a known recipe from buffers | 1–12 units |
| T3 Hatchery line | 4 | Incubator clutches, hatchling chute and sorter, rack routing | Hatchlings go by species rules to racks and tanks | Units |
| T4 Analyzer | 4–5 | Analyzer and planner (research bench L3–L4) | Points at undiscovered mixes and pairs; the player still chooses | Units |

- **Power** is a simple budget: generators supply units, each running machine draws units, a panel shows supply and draw. Over budget, machines run slower in a set priority (shown), never stop silently or break. No fuel chores after the hand crank.
- **Pipes** are a logical graph laid with the existing placement system on the lab grid (no fluid physics; §5 kept): a grower's tap → pump → buffer → incubator port. AD-D's glowing culture pipes become these pipes.
- **Staff layer on top** (GR.10): Pip serves, Nori stocks, Moss runs known recipes and known breeding pairs. Neither machines nor staff ever choose an undiscovered mix or pair.
- **The R08 systems are re-homed, not lost:** the collector module becomes T2 pumps and buffers; the doser module becomes the auto-doser; desired stock targets (R08.3) drive the T3 line and Moss. Job ownership, reservations, blocked reasons and recovery (§6.6) stay as they are.
- **End state:** a known creature can go from free cultures through the line to a paid sale for a whole shift without repeated clicks (§6.6 goal kept), and the analyzer points the player at what to discover next.

### GR.9 — Stations and upgrades

Every station has levels. Each level changes its look and adds a capability, a capacity step or a verb. Upgrades are bought on the station's card (an Upgrade tab, showing the next level's change, cost and gate); they install at once when the station is idle. Costs: credits (physical) and sometimes RP (research-led); first-pass cost curve base × 2.5 per level, set in GR-01.

| Station | L1 | L2 | L3 | L4 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Siphon / net (tool) | Hand bulb: 3 doses, hold 0.8 s each; picks creatures in peek | Bigger bulb: 6 doses, 0.6 s | Twin chamber: 10, collects 2 at a time | Vacuum: 16, pulls from every grower in range while held |
| Growers (each culture) | Capacity 4, refill 3 s | 6, 2 s | 10, 1.5 s; tap for a pipe | 16, 1 s; feeds two pipes |
| Incubator | 1 creature, 12 s, mixes of 2–3 | 8 s, mixes up to 4, mix-log screen | Clutch of 2, mixes up to 6, tint port | Clutch of 3, auto-doser port (needs power) |
| Breeding tank | 1 pair, 60 s | 40 s, tint port, Large parents | 2 pairs | Planner link |
| Starter / Large / Show tank | GR.5 capacities by level | Filter (+spaces) | Lid / water type (comfort) | Show spotlight (Show tank) |
| Lab rack | 4 spaces | 8 | 12 | — |
| Counter | Bell: hold 1.2 s to bag | Bag dispenser: 0.8 s | Card reader: 0.5 s, queue of 3 | Second till (Pip serves while you do too) |
| Research bench | Notes: culture icons on silhouettes | Microscope: distance meter | Analyzer: one undiscovered reachable mix or pair a day | Planner: predicts any known pair |
| Conditioning vat | Mist process (as today) | Double batch | Faster; Shimmer tint | — |
| Generator | Hand crank (1 unit while cranked) | Bubble turbine (3) | Twin turbine (6) | Tide engine (12) |
| Pumps | Hand pump | Electric pump (1 unit, slow) | Fast pump (2 units) | — |

The AD-E hands-on checkout becomes the counter's L1; Serve-by-button stays retired once the counter is a station with levels.

### GR.10 — Shop level, stars, customers and helpers

- **Stars** (AD-E): 1–5 from reputation (thresholds rescaled to the new length in GR-01). Reputation rises with sales and granted wishes, falls with refusals, impatience and slaps (§6.2 kept). Stars gate expansions, catalog pages, customer types and helpers.
- **Customers** (AD-E kept): types unlock by stars; wishes (species, trait, now also "a pair" or "a school of three" from 3★); themes (decor near a container); Big spenders favour Medium and Large creatures. Visitors browse a container and choose a specimen; one reservation per specimen.
- **Helpers are earned** (gate = stars + one achievement), start at level 1 and train:

| Helper | Gate | Level 1 | Training (credits, at night) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Pip (cashier) | 2★ and 40 sales | Serves in 3 s | L2 2 s, L3 1.2 s and calls the next visitor |
| Nori (stocker) | 3★ and a Large tank owned | Moves one creature at a time | L2 two at a time, L3 follows schools |
| Moss (technician) | 3★ and a powered generator | Runs one known recipe | L2 known breeding pairs, L3 two jobs |

  Each helper has a hire fee and a wage that rises with level (first pass: fee 40/60/80, wages 6–12 a duty period; GR-01). Wages keep §6.3's paid-duty rules.

#### GR.10.1 — Economy, first pass (GR-01 replaces with modelled values: see GR.19.1)

| Item | First-pass value |
|---|---|
| Standard price by size | Small 6–14, Medium 24–40, Large 70–140 credits (family and era raise it) |
| Starter / Large / Show tank | 20 / 90 / 300 credits; levels base × 2.5 |
| Shop units B / C | 150 / 600 credits plus the star gate |
| Lab annex / workshop | 250 / 700 credits plus the star gate |
| Station upgrades | L2 about 15–40, L3 about 60–150, L4 about 250–500 credits; research-led levels also cost RP |
| Research | Spark 10 RP, breeding tank 5 RP, Mist 25 RP, analyzer L3 30 RP, Dusk 40 RP |
| Visitors at once | 2 (unit A), 3 (B), 4 (C), 5 (final); counter queue 3 |
| Schools and themes | +15 and +25 tolerance points, capped together at +40 |
| Helpers | Hire 40 / 60 / 80; wage 6 at L1 rising to 12 at L3 a duty period |

The first-hour economy must make the first upgrade affordable within 5 minutes and the second tank within about 15, with every era reachable from a zero-money start using free Bloom and Flow (§6.3 kept).

### GR.11 — Spaces and level design

The rooms are rebuilt as a real shop and a real lab: deep rather than wide, dense, with front-to-back meaning.

```
Era 1 (unit A + back room)                          +z (north)
+--------------------------[ door ]------------------+   back room 6 x 5 m:
| [tank wall: 2 starter tank places]                 |   growers on the left wall,
|                                                    |   incubator centre, rack,
|        [centre island place]                       |   research pinboard
|                                                    |
| [supplies shelf]                 [counter][queue]  |
+----[window display]---------[ entrance ]-----------+   -z (street)
            shop unit A: 8 x 8 m
```

| Tier | Shop | Lab | Container places (approx.) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 (start) | Unit A 8 × 8 m | Back room 6 × 5 m | 4 tank places, 2 rack places |
| 2 (2★) | + Unit B east: 16 × 8 m, wall opened with an arch | Back room | 8 tank places |
| 3 (3★) | 16 × 8 m | + Lab annex 8 × 8 m behind (power room corner) | 8, plus lab racks and breeding |
| 4 (4★) | + Unit C north-east: L-shape 16 × 12 m | + Workshop 8 × 6 m (generator, line) | 14, incl. Show tank walls |
| 5 (5★) | Exhibition hall finish (lighting, banners) | Final | 16 + exhibition displays |

- **Proportions:** ceilings 3.2 m (shop) and 3.6 m (lab, for overhead pipes). Rooms no shallower than 6 m. Main aisle 1.6 m (two visitors abreast); browse access 1.2 m in front of every container; counter queue 3 places within the shop.
- **Readability:** from the entrance, the counter and a feature wall are visible; every container's front faces an aisle; containers sit at 0.9–1.6 m for looking in. The shop has shelves of supplies, price cards, a window display and a feature wall; the lab has benches, wall plumbing, a power board and a pinboard. AD-D's look (toon, set pieces, pipes) carries over; its set pieces move into the new units.
- **Expansions** are "open the wall into the next unit" moments at night (a short reveal), not stretching one strip. The AD-D set pieces become unit features (bay window bench in unit B, feature tank on unit C's wall, pipe gallery in the annex, dome in the workshop).
- **Navigation and placement rules** (§6.4) are kept: snapped footprints, access points, validated paths, no trapped actors; the placement system lays containers, decor, racks, machines and pipes.

### GR.12 — Objective board and the ending

- The **objective board** (a pinboard in the back room, mirrored in the journal) always shows the current era's goal, the next unlock and its gate, and the long goal: "Discover creatures: n / 72 · Journal: n / 288 · Exhibition: requirements".
- **Grand Exhibition** (§4 kept, numbers updated): available after the final shop and lab tiers, **48 of 72 species** across all nine families, **any 5 of the 9 crowns**, and one full shift with a working line from cultures to sale. Exhibition displays, six announced purchases, retry without loss and free play afterwards are kept from §4.

### GR.13 — Interface changes (UX.x rules apply)

Container card (occupant grid of space icons, schools, comfort), peek labels, incubator card (dose slots by level, mix log, "close to something new", distance meter), breeding tank card (parents, timer, tint, predicted result with the planner), Upgrade tab on every station card, power board (supply vs draw, priorities), pipe placement mode, supplier catalog, objective board, journal pages (Cultures, Families with silhouettes and hints, Mix log, Pair log and chart, colours). All in the UI Toolkit system (UX-02–UX-09) and the cozy style; the HUD keeps at most three persistent elements.

### GR.14 — Art and audio

- Scale AD-B creatures to their size class (small ones about half today's size) and add the 1.3× head ratio; check readability in peek.
- New family rigs for Cinderling, Reefback and Gloamer (Blender, local, original; AD rules), species meshes per family, colour atlas per AD-B.
- Container models per tier and level (filters, lids, spotlights), station models per level (3–4 looks each), generator and pump models, pipe pieces; units A–C and the lab annex and workshop rebuilt in the AD look.
- Sounds: cranking, pumps, generator hum (ambience bus), breeding "pop", school swish; comfort settings scale every new moment (AD.7 rule).

### GR.15 — Technical plan

- **Build alongside, then cut over.** GR is built as a new ruleset in the same project behind a development switch (as AD-E), with its own fresh save line, so the current game and its validation stay intact until GR reaches parity (GR-14), when the classic ruleset and the routes that encode it retire.
- **Saves:** a new save format version for GR. A save from the classic game is never overwritten: GR offers a new shop and keeps the old slot as a backup (§7 save contract kept). No migration of classic saves (user decision, GR.17).
- **Core data model** (pure, tested without scenes; §7 kept):
  - `CultureDefinition` list (id, colour, source, unlock), replacing the fixed `CultureKind` enum.
  - `SpeciesDefinition` gains size class, breeding value, founder recipe (multiset) or special pair, temperament and water.
  - Containers hold ordered specimen lists with space accounting; reservations per specimen (no longer per tank).
  - Station levels (`StationUpgrades`), power grid (supply, draw, priorities), pipe graph (nodes on the lab grid), breeding tank, research points, supplier orders, objective state, helper levels.
- **Definitions as data:** tuning and content tables (cultures, species, costs, gates, eras) in one place, loaded at start and validated (unknown IDs, unreachable unlocks, cost overflow).
- **Pacing simulator** (GR-01): a deterministic, Core-only bot that plays the economy at accelerated time with simple policies, reporting when each unlock and era is reached, money and RP over time, and dead ends. Every tuning change reruns it; its report is evidence.
- **Validation:** EditMode tests for every rule (mix resolution, breeding, capacity, schools, power, pipes, gates, upgrades, saves); Windows routes per era from fixture saves at 1280 × 720 and 1920 × 1080 (captures reviewed); performance routes for the densest layout; human playtest gates (GR.16).

#### GR.15.1 — Rules and edge cases (each needs a test)

- **Capacity:** a hatchling or offspring waits in its machine's output until there is space (output reserved before cultures are used, §6.5 kept); the card says which containers have room. A container never accepts a creature that does not fit, from the player, staff or the line.
- **Reservations:** visitors reserve one specimen each; a wish for a pair or school reserves several at once, all or none; moving or selling a reserved specimen is refused with its reason; closing releases them.
- **Breeding:** parents in the breeding tank are not for sale and can be taken out at any time (the timer resets); offspring never replace stock; a pair whose result would not fit waits.
- **Mixing:** an empty mix is refused; a mix larger than the incubator's level allows is refused before doses are used; owning a culture is checked when dosing, and the nearest-founder search only considers owned cultures.
- **Power and pipes:** a disconnected or blocked pipe, an empty buffer or a full destination shows a blocked reason and pauses that line only; removing a pipe or machine at night releases its jobs safely; a save mid-job restores the job or releases it, never duplicates culture or creatures.
- **Upgrades:** an upgrade installs only when its station is idle, keeps every creature and culture inside it, and never lowers capacity below what the station holds.
- **Expansions and placement:** rearranging at night only (§6.1 kept); a stocked container moves with its occupants in one validated transaction (§6.4 kept).
- **Gates:** every gate shows its requirement and progress; nothing required for the ending can become unreachable (sold the last of a species → breeding or mixing can always make it again).
- **Comfort:** peek, schools and new moments follow Reduced motion and Low detail; peek can be switched off.

### GR.16 — Risks and mitigations

| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| Art scope (72 species in nine families, 6 new family rigs, station levels) | Family rigs, species by mesh and colour (AD.3), placeholder-first greybox, content in waves |
| Many creatures on screen | GR.5 budget, LOD and update spreading, measured early in GR-03 |
| Pipes and power become fiddly | Logical pipes only, short lab grid, auto-routing suggestion; power is a budget, not a simulation |
| The curve is outrun (the hole game's criticism) | Era gates are stars plus an achievement, not money alone; pacing simulator; playtests |
| Small creatures unreadable | Peek, names on focus, head ratio, schools; checked in GR-02 |
| Test churn | Build alongside; classic routes retire only at cut-over |
| Grind | Never-fail mixing, deterministic breeding, analyzer hints, RP from discovery |

### GR.17 — Decisions (accepted by the user, 2026-10-23)

1. Goal: the collection; the shop funds it. 2. Length: about 4 h to the ending, 8–12 h for everything. 3. Fresh start for the redesign. 4. Starting tank 4 spaces, upgradable to 6 and 8; schools sell better. 5. Three size classes (1, 2 and 4 spaces) and three tank types. 6. Mixing allowed; comfort bonuses later, never failures. 7. Smaller creatures with a peek view. 8. Automation through power and pipes, tiers T0–T4. 9. Automation order: collection, then dosing, then moving, then checkout; discovery never. 10. The player ends as inventor and director. 11. Seven cultures from growers, the catalog and research, locked slots visible. 12. Both mixing (founders) and breeding (hybrids). 13. A fixed authored collection (about 36 species, 144 entries; **raised to 72 species in nine families of eight, 288 entries, at GR-01's review**) with Palworld-style predictable pairs and special pairs; no luck for required entries. 14. Never fail; hints and a distance meter. 15. Proportions are the main problem of the rooms. 16. References: *TCG Card Shop Simulator*, *Supermarket Simulator*, real pet shops. 17. Start in a small square shop with a back-room lab; expand by opening walls. 18. Helpers earned by stars plus an achievement, starting slow and trainable; first after about an hour. 19. Every station has 3–5 levels that change look and capability. 20. GR replaces AD-F and absorbs AD-E (stars as shop level, wishes, themes, hands-on checkout, forecast; day length pending the AD-E playtest).

Decided at GR-01's review (2026-10-23): 72 species in nine families of eight; the curve, the mixing clarification (GR.19.2) and the late-game numbers accepted ("the rest seems ok").

Open for the user later (not blocking GR-01): final names (cultures, families, species), the exact numbers (set by GR-01 and playtests), whether juvenile growth stages are wanted (out of scope now: they add waiting), and the exhibition's final shape.

### GR.19 — GR-01 record: content, rules, pacing model and tuning (2026-10-23)

**Built (engine-free, `Assets/EvolutionShop/Core/Growth`, namespace `EvolutionShop.Growth`):**
- `GrowthContent.cs`: cultures (GR.6), the nine families and 72 species (GR.7: recipe or special pair, breeding value, price, temperament, water; size by family), the intended breeding pair of every hybrid, research points, every station's levels with cost, gate and stats (GR.9), containers and their levels (GR.5), spaces (GR.11), helpers (GR.10), stars, eras with their target windows (GR.4), the exhibition requirements (GR.12) and visitor types. Every number is data; this file is the single source for GR's numbers.
- `GrowthRules.cs`: `Mix` (GR.7.1, as clarified in GR.19.2), `Breed` (GR.7.2: nearest non-crown breeding value to the parents' average rounded up, ties to the lower; special pairs make crowns; same species breeds true), `BreedColour`, `Fits` (spaces and largest size), and `Validate`: unique ids, recipes and breeding values; every founder's own recipe makes it; recipes within their era's incubator; crowns with two different parents; every hybrid's intended pair breeds it; every species reachable no later than its family's era (hybrids and crowns from era 2, when the breeding tank arrives); the exhibition reachable.
- `PacingSimulator.cs`: a deterministic first-time player. Nights: pay wages, buy (saving for the next gate purchase, buying only small things meanwhile; a Starter tank swapped for the Large tank when the floor is full), try new mixes and pairs (expected tries per discovery set by the research bench's level), chase colours, fill the shop's free spaces toward the day's demand by hand (Moss and, later, the line add theirs). Days (5:30): visitors arrive by stars and space, each buying the most valuable creature in stock within budget; reputation from sales, wishes and refusals. It reports every GR.3/GR.4 target, a timeline and a day-by-day table. `PacingPolicy` holds its assumptions about the player.
- `GrowthReport.cs` and `Editor/GrowthReports.cs`: `Docs/Evidence/gr-01/` ROSTER.md, PACING.md and pacing-curve.svg (menu **Evolution Shop › GR › Write roster and pacing report**; batch `-executeMethod EvolutionShop.Editor.GrowthReports.WriteGr01`), plus the hand-written REVIEW.md for the user.
- Tests: `GrowthContentTests` (validation, nine families of eight with three founders, four hybrids and a crown, three of each size; every one of the 1,708 mixes of 2–6 doses makes a founder; known-founder resolution and the one-dose hint; every pair of the 72 × 72 breeds predictably and never past the later parent's era; crowns only from their pair; every hybrid some pair's offspring; colour, spaces, station levels, stars) and `PacingSimulatorTests` (deterministic; the typical player meets every target with no dead end; eras, helpers and the ending in order; careful and quick players finish; the documents cover every species and the SVG parses).

#### GR.19.0 — The user's review (2026-10-23)

The first roster had 36 species. The user: "About the roster 36 seems like a small number, the rest seems ok"; asked how many, chose **72 species: nine families of eight** (recommended over 60, 72 in six bigger families, and 100+). Each family: three founders, four hybrids, one crown; three small, three medium and three large families. New families: **Dewling** (small, Mist, era 3), **Saltscale** (medium, Brine, era 4), **Cometail** (large, Spark and Dusk, era 5); the six others gained a founder and a hybrid each (Brookkin two hybrids). Each family has its own breeding-value band, the bands in era order (Brookkin 40–160 up to Cometail 1630–1800), and the first hybrid of each family is bred from the family before it (for example Kelpgrazer = Mistlet × Shellback). The curve, the mixing clarification (GR.19.2) and the late-game numbers stand as accepted. Still open: the user's look at the 72-species roster (new families and names).

The user approved the 72-species roster and the new families on 2026-10-23 ("That's good continue for gr-02"): GR-01's gate passed.

| Spaces | unit B 150, annex 250, unit C 600, workshop 700 | unit B 400, annex 1,000, unit C 4,000, workshop 5,000, exhibition hall 6,000 | As above; the hall is about a day's takings at 5★ |

| Stations L3/L4 | about 60–150 / 250–500 | e.g. incubator 25 / 250 / 3,000; generator 200 / 800 / 3,000 / 6,000; pumps 150 / 400 / 1,500; sorter 3,000; research bench 80 / 1,500 / 4,000 | Gate items (breeding tank, generator and pumps to L2, sorter, incubator L4, analyzer) are what the player saves for |

#### GR.19.2 — Clarification: mixing (accepted with the review, 2026-10-23)

GR.7.1 says a non-exact mix makes "the founder whose recipe is nearest". Read literally, any mix near an undiscovered founder would make it, so "one dose from something new" and the microscope would point at nothing and the needle hunt would disappear. **Built:** an exact recipe makes its founder, new or known; any other mix makes the nearest founder the player already knows (the very first mix, before anything is known, makes the nearest of all); the result says when the mix was one dose from something new. Every mix still makes a creature.

#### GR.19.3 — The curve (`Docs/Evidence/gr-01/PACING.md`)

| Player | Era 2 | Era 3 | Era 4 | Era 5 | First helper | Powered | Exhibition | Collect-all |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Typical (all 12 targets met) | 0:24 | 1:20 | 2:11 | 3:26 | 1:20 | 1:50 | 3:39 | 8:19 |
| Careful | 0:30 | 1:19 | 2:21 | 3:42 | 1:19 | 1:59 | 4:03 | 8:58 |
| Quick | 0:23 | 1:05 | 1:59 | 3:15 | 1:05 | 1:44 | 3:29 | 8:28 |

Something new arrives at least every 9 minutes before the exhibition (typical), a species about every 3 minutes; the typical player finds the 72nd species at about 3:50. 1,708 mixes all land on a founder; every hybrid is the offspring of some pair, each crown of exactly one.

#### GR.19.4 — Findings, known limits and what the GR-02 playtest must measure

- **Soft lock found by the model:** a floor full of Starter tanks left no place for the Large tank that era 3 needs. The game must let containers be stored or sold (classic furniture storage carries over; GR-03, GR-10).
- The policy's guesses: 4-minute nights, 3 new mixes a night, 4 new pairs a day, a quarter of the day free while serving alone (0.6 with Pip, 0.85 with Pip and Nori), 30% of sales granting a wish, 2 colours a night before the exhibition and 4 after.
- Era 2 is the hands-only stretch by design: before Pip about 20 visitors a day leave unserved for want of stock. The playtest must say whether it reads as pressure or grind.
- Collect-all depends mostly on colours chased a night; a rule (one tint per port a night) could make it hold by design if playtests show it too fast.
- The model stocks only what visitors buy (at most about 90 creatures shown); real players fill tanks. The final shop has 184 spaces, at GR.5's 180-creature budget: GR-03 measures.
- A founder at the top of its family's band (for example Nebulafin) is made only by mixing; breeding reaches every hybrid but not every founder.

### GR.20 — GR-02 record: the greybox core prototype (2026-10-23)

The user approved GR-01's 72-species roster ("That's good continue for gr-02"); GR-02 is built beside the classic game (GR.15) and validated in numbered run 1 (`Docs/Evidence/gr-02-VALIDATION.md`). Its gate is the user's playtest (`Docs/Evidence/gr-02/PLAY.md`).

**Built:**
- `Core/Growth/GrowthShop.cs` (engine-free; `GrowthShopTests`, 16): growers that hold and refill doses; the siphon, filled one dose per hold time while the mouse is held on a grower; the incubator (doses in and out, never-fail mixing as GR.19.2, the hatchling waiting in the output, the mix log with counts and near misses, `Preview`'s "one dose from something new"); carrying up to 3; containers with spaces, occupants and schools of 3; visitors by GrowthContent's weights and stars who browse 6 s, reserve the best creature within budget (a school's species +15% budget) and queue with 40 s of patience; serving by holding at the counter; 5:30 days, untimed nights, the day summary; reputation (+2 a sale, −1 when a visitor finds nothing to buy or tires of waiting) and stars; era 2 at 2★, 3 creatures and 2 tanks; Starter tanks on free places; shop unit B (400 credits, at night, era 2); siphon, grower, incubator and counter upgrades to level 2; `Snapshot`/`Restore` (`GrowthSave` version 1, validated on load).
- `Runtime/Growth`: `GrowthSession` (the switch `GrowthGreybox`, camera and player, focus by the reticle, peek, hold actions, autosave every 5 s, `growth-gr02.json`), `GrowthWorld` (unit A 8 × 8 m, 3.2 m ceiling, window and street door; back room 6 × 5 m, 3.6 m ceiling; unit B 8 × 8 m behind its partition; stations, tanks, place markers, the counter's queue and visitor routes), `GrowthCreatureView` (placeholder creatures at size class: small 7.5 cm with a 1.3× head; Bubblebelly round, Ribbonfin long-tailed; recipe colours kept saturated; visitors as capsules by type), `GrowthUi` (HUD, cards, journal, receipt and pause screens, peek tag), `GrowthSmoke` (the route). `Editor/GrowthGreyboxMenu.cs`: **Evolution Shop › GR › Play the GR-02 greybox**. `ShopPlayer.Pitch`; `ShopSession.Awake` hands over when asked.

**GR-02's choices, for the user to overrule:** visitors arrive every `ArrivalSeconds` (6 s at 1★) while fewer than `VisitorsAtOnce` are in (2 in unit A, +1 with unit B); an empty shop draws nobody in and costs nothing (counted as walked past); leaving visitors take 8 s; the reticle reaches 3.2 m and peek starts within 1.6 m of the glass, easing the view from 68° to 45° in 0.25 s (instant with reduced motion, switchable off); E on a tank puts in what is carried, E in peek takes the creature under the reticle; stations stop at level 2 in the greybox; unit B is bought at night only; the receipt opens at the day's end; Start over has no confirmation.

**Found while building:** a first console day had about 50 refusals as visitors kept coming to an empty shop (now the walk-past rule); the reticle's ray met the player's own capsule (the player is on Ignore Raycast); looks fixed from the route's captures (light leak over the back-room door, window, glare, unreadable and overlapping HUD labels, the incubator card's refresh, journal rows, peek's duplicate label, grey mixed colours). Details in the validation document.

**Validation (run 1):** EditMode 2,493 of 2,493; development player `gr02-greybox-1`; the GR-02 route passed at 1280×720 and 1920×1080 (119 checks each, 23 captures, average frame 8.5 ms); the classic cycle (123), HUD (89) and card (170) routes passed.

**The user's playtest (2026-10-23):** "Feel good for now continue to next gr task": starting tiny and the small creatures were accepted as they are; GR-02's gate passed. No numbers changed.

**For the playtest to answer (GR.19.4's measures included):** does starting tiny feel right (collecting, the 12 s incubator, carrying, serving, the 5:30 day, the time to era 2 and unit B); are small creatures readable in the tank, as a school and in peek; does the hands-only stretch read as pressure or grind.

### GR.33 — GR-12 wave 4 record: the Gloamer and the Cometail in the AD look (2026-10-24)

At the user's word ("checked both its great continue for the next", which approved waves 2 and 3), GR-12's wave 4, the last, is built and validated in numbered run 6 (`Docs/Evidence/gr-12-VALIDATION.md`); its gate is **the user's look** (`Docs/Evidence/gr-12/LOOK.md`). The user chose (2026-10-24) the families' looks: a **glowing lure** for the Gloamer (big, dark, gentle deep-sea fish with a glowing lure dangling from the brow on a curved stalk and soft glowing spots along the sides; its trick a lure) and a **comet tail** for the Cometail (sleek fish with a bright glowing head and a long tail streaming behind like a comet's, in glowing wisps; its trick a streak). With wave 4 all 72 species of GR.7's roster are made.

**Built:**
- `Tools/Art/create_diorama_kit.py`: 16 species builders on the shared rig (`lure` on the Lantern pivot and `glow_spots` for the Gloamer; `comet_head` and `comet_tail` with `swell_tube` for the Cometail), their colours (each Classic clear of its family's new water; a near-black body's Deep a jewel tone), the families' review sheets; the 56 earlier models proved identical and kept; the atlas full.
- `Presentation`: `DioramaFamily.Gloamer` and `Cometail` with their waters and fluids, 16 `Wave` rows (72 kit species), `DioramaTricks` (lure, streak, eclipse, starfall; `Releases`; the Gloamer's breathing lights and winking lure, hanging nose down; the Cometail's flickering glow and tail flick), `DioramaLook.GlowSlot` (both families), `CreatureVisual` (`DioramaStroke` and the families' strokes; `DioramaTailPivot`).
- `Runtime/Growth`: `GrowthCreatureView.Burst` (motes from a Gloamer's lure, stardust from a Cometail's tail), `GrowthCreatureBurst` (glowing motes drifting slowly, glittering stardust), `GrowthStarts` (`KitFamilies` with all nine, `NewestFamilies`; the creatures start point reworked), the creatures route over four waves (now 122 checks; its containment message more telling).
- Tests: `DioramaCreatureTests` over the 72 kit species and nine families (the lure, the eclipse and the starfall, the new habits, the nine waters, the full atlas), `GrowthKitCreatureTests` (colours, glow, tricks, bursts from the lure and the tail, the crowns grander, every trick inside every container's water, the habit on top).

**GR-12 wave 4's choices, for the user to overrule:** the 16 designs and colours; the families' waters (a deep twilight indigo for the Gloamer, a warm violet sky for the Cometail); the lure and streak and the crowns' eclipse and starfall with their lengths (1.1 to 2 s) and tempos (0.75 to 1.3); the Gloamer's dark bodies; the Cometail's whole head glowing; the habits and strokes; motes from the lure and stardust from the tail; the start point (wave 4 in two colours, the approved waves in Classic, two families to a tank).

**Found while building:** the first review sheets (the Cometails' tails thin spikes and their heads ragged patches, the Gloamer's spots too small, the Gloomfin's fans edge-on, the Nightlantern's lures bunched), each redrawn; the Eclipse Leviathan's eclipse 1.2 cm too high for a Large tank's water and the Starmaw's streak 0.9 cm past its depth (the lure lower, the tails shorter); the Celestial Wyrm, slender, taking less room than three of its family (fuller, dragon-wing fins); one scratch route measuring the Duskdrifter's tail 0.4 cm past its water, within the allowance and not again (its tail and the Cometails' a little shorter for margin).

### GR.34 — GR-12B decisions: a body plan for every species (2026-10-24)

At the look at GR-12's wave 4 the user said: "something that bothers me is that most of them have the same face and body, you only added accessories to different ones, lets think about how we can fix it, you can take inspiration from real life aquatic creatures not only fishes, manta rays, sharks and more and even aquatic dinos". Claude found the cause in `Tools/Art/create_diorama_kit.py`: 59 species are built by `loft_creature` (58 on `nose_profile`'s round nose and smooth taper) and 10 by `round_creature`, every one with `creature_face` (lidded eyes, blush and pouty lips), so species differ by proportions, colours and add-ons; and `CreatureVisual.DioramaStroke` swims every species by its family's roll, pitch and tail.

**The user's decisions (2026-10-24):**

| Question | Decision |
|---|---|
| Which species | **All 72**, AD-B's five included. |
| Which animals | **Anything aquatic:** fish of every shape, rays, sharks, eels and serpents, turtles, jellies, squid and nautilus, crabs, sea slugs, frogs and salamanders, sea mammals (otter, manatee, walrus, penguin) and aquatic dinosaurs (plesiosaur, ichthyosaur, mosasaur, Dunkleosteus, Spinosaurus). |
| Faces | **The house eyes stay** (big, glossy, blinking, AD.4), placed to suit the animal (on the sides, on top, forward); the mouth varies (a beak, a toothy grin, a tube snout, a wide smile, whiskers); pouty lips and blush only where they suit. |
| Start | **A pilot: the Gloamer's eight as Blender review sheets** for the user's look, then family by family. |

**The principle:** a family is its signature (the Gloamer's lure and lights, the Cometail's comet tail, and so on), its colours, its water and its trick; a species is its own animal: body plan, face and way of swimming (rays and turtles flap, eels and serpents undulate, jellies pulse, squid jet, axolotls, frogs and crabs paddle, dolphins, whales and marine reptiles beat a fluke, seahorses swim upright). Every model keeps the rig's part and pivot names, so the game's code still finds them; new joints (a tail in segments, say) are added where an animal needs them.

**Claude's draft of who becomes what** (open for the user's changes; the names stay):

| Family | Draft |
|---|---|
| Brookkin | Pondlet a dwarf gourami, Bubblebelly a fancy goldfish (a ryukin) and Ribbonfin a freshwater angelfish (AD-B's, the user's choice of 2026-10-25); Puddlepup an axolotl, Splashkit an otter kit, Reedwhisker a catfish, Lilyfin a frog, Brook Monarch a baby plesiosaur; every Brookkin keeps the house's pouty lips and blush (the user's choice of 2026-10-25) |
| Sparkline | Lanternfin a lanternfish (AD-B's, the user's choice of 2026-10-25); Sparkminnow a minnow, Fizzlet a jellyfish, Glowbelly a firefly squid, Flickerfin an electric eel, Buzzgill a shrimp, Starglimmer a sea angel, Aurorafin a rainbow comb jelly |
| Airfin | Driftwing an African butterflyfish (AD-B's, the user's choice of 2026-10-25); Puffglider a flying gurnard, Hazewing a butterfly ray, Gustgill a flying fish, Mistrunner a mudskipper, Cloudcarp a carp (a sailfish, the user's choice of 2026-10-25, since wave 2 made the Cometail's Cosmocarp a koi), Breezeling a leafy sea dragon, Zephyray a great manta |
| Cinderling | Emberpuff a pufferfish, Cindertail a fire salamander, Coalbelly a frogfish, Sizzlesnout a gharial, Kindlekoi a koi (a betta, the user's choice of 2026-10-24, since wave 2 made the Cometail's Cosmocarp a koi), Sootsnap a snapping turtle, Ashfin a lionfish, Phoenix Fry a baby Spinosaurus with a flame sail |
| Dewling | Dewdrop a sea bunny, Fogfin a sea butterfly, Mistlet a tadpole, Drizzler a nudibranch, Pearlpip a swimming scallop, Haloling a moon jelly, Rainskipper a flying squid, Rainbow Regent a Spanish dancer sea slug |
| Reefback | Shellback a sea turtle, Coralhorn a cowfish, Clamjaw a grouper, Kelpgrazer a manatee, Barnaclebrow a grey whale calf, Tidetusk a walrus, Wavebreaker a hammerhead, Coralossus a giant decorator crab wearing a reef |
| Saltscale | Brinefin a mackerel, Pebblejaw a parrotfish, Seaspark a banded sea snake, Foamtail a dolphin, Surfskipper a penguin, Shoalglider an eagle ray, Tidepip a seahorse, Pearl Emperor an ichthyosaur |
| Gloamer | Moonmouth an anglerfish, Gloomfin a sunfish, Shadeback a coelacanth, Hushray a stingray, Duskdrifter an oarfish, Murkmaw a viperfish, Nightlantern a vampire squid, Eclipse Leviathan a mosasaur |
| Cometail | Starmaw a whale shark, Meteorback a Dunkleosteus, Nebulafin a cuttlefish, Orbitail an ammonite, Quasarray a sawfish, Novatusk a narwhal, Cosmocarp a carp, Celestial Wyrm a true sea serpent |

AD-B's five are drawn as animals of their own too (the user chose all 72, and on 2026-10-25 their animals: each suits the trick it plays, a gourami's bubbles, a goldfish's balloon, an angelfish's twirl, a lanternfish's flash, a butterflyfish's loop); the classic game keeps showing them, so their redesign reaches it too.

**What it changes:** AD.3's rule that a family's species differ only by mesh, signature and habit (noted there); the kit script gains body-plan helpers (a loft along a curved spine, a disc, a shell with a neck, a bell, a mantle with arms, limbs) and a face helper with mouths of several kinds on the same Lips shape keys; `CreatureVisual` gains a way of swimming per species; each species' tricks, water fit (GR.32) and the crowns' size rule keep their tests.

**The pilot (2026-10-24, Claude):** the Gloamer's eight built as GR.34's draft animals with new body-plan helpers (`Tools/Art/bodyplan_pilot/`) and rendered beside the current eight (`ArtSource/DioramaKit/GR12B_pilot/`; review note `Docs/Evidence/gr-12b/LOOK.md`). Claude's first sheets showed four problems, each redrawn: the oarfish's back fin dipped to nothing behind its head (a helper's easing ran the wrong way) and its sharp S-bends read as boxes (now a gentle wave, one ribbon), its trailing fins hung down like legs (now along its belly); the anglerfish's mouth vanished from the side (a jaw line now runs back along each cheek, its chin smaller); the coelacanth's lobed fins read as grape clusters (each now one tapered lobe with a fan); and the viperfish's fangs hardly showed (longer, its jaw deeper). Each keeps its four colours for now.

### GR.35 — GR-12B wave 1 record: the Gloamer in game, swimming by body plan (2026-10-24)

The user approved GR-12B's pilot on 2026-10-24 ("it looks really good continue"); wave 1 puts the Gloamer's eight in game and teaches remade species to swim by their body plans. Validated in numbered run 1 (`Docs/Evidence/gr-12b-VALIDATION.md`); its gate is **the user's look in game** (`Docs/Evidence/gr-12b/LOOK.md`).

**Built:**
- `Tools/Art/create_diorama_kit.py`: a GR-12B section with the pilot's body-plan helpers and `bp_wave_keys`, and the Gloamer's eight from the pilot under their old builders' names (an anglerfish, an ocean sunfish with a Keel pivot, a coelacanth, a stingray, an oarfish with four Wave keys, a viperfish, a vampire squid, a mosasaur); the vampire squid's mouth moved onto its mantle's front under its eyes. The other 64 models and every other render rebuilt identical and kept their committed bytes. The pilot's scripts (`Tools/Art/bodyplan_pilot/`) are retired; its approved sheets stay in `ArtSource/DioramaKit/GR12B_pilot/`.
- `Presentation`: `DioramaGait` (Family, Flap, Scull, Undulate, Pulse) and `DioramaSpecies.Remade` and `Gait` (the remade species and how each swims); `CreatureVisual`: the Keel pivot, the Wave keys with the tail riding them (`TailWave`), the cloak's pulse, `DioramaGaitStroke`, `DioramaStroke` giving a remade species its gait's strokes, and `DioramaBodyLength` a remade species' span (`Span`).
- `Runtime/Growth/GrowthCreatureView.Envelope`: a remade species' swim room samples its crest's and keel's sway, its cloak at its widest pulse, its body's box grown by its wave (`WaveReach`) and its tail fin riding the wave.
- Tests: `RemadeSpeciesSwimByTheirBodyPlans`; the rig test by body plan; `ItsSwimRoomCoversItsWayOfSwimming`; the size test by span for remade species; the crown test by span for a remade family.
- `Docs/Evidence/gr-12b/Invoke-GR12B.ps1` (GR-12's steps, its evidence in `gr-12b`).

**Wave 1's choices, for the user to overrule:** sizing by span (each remade species its class's size across its widest extent, not counting a lure; the round ones read a little smaller than GR-12's round fish); the crown spanning more than its family rather than taking the most room (a slender mosasaur cannot out-bulk a round anglerfish); the four ways of swimming with their sizes and speeds (a ray's beat of 24 degrees about every 5 s, a sunfish's sweep of 22, an oarfish's wave of 5.5 cm in the model at its tail, a squid's pulse of 12%); the four others keeping the Gloamer's strokes; the colours kept from GR-12.

**Found while building:** the vampire squid's mouth too far back on its upright mantle for the rig's "lips at the front" (moved onto the mantle's front, and a remade species' lips need be 0.15 m, not 0.2 m, ahead of its body's middle); two new tests measuring a creature's rest just after it was built, when it had already swum one frame (they set it at rest first); and sizing by the body's length, which would have made the squid enormous and let no slender crown out-bulk a round fish (hence sizing by span).

**Validation (run 1):** EditMode 2,587 of 2,587; development player `gr12b-bodyplans-1` (224.3 MB); the GR-12 creatures route (122 checks) at both sizes, every kit creature inside its water through 8,498 frames each (0.0 cm past at worst); the GR-10 spaces (60; the final layout 5.59 ms average uncapped), GR-09 helpers (98), GR-08 line (77), GR-07 power (91), GR-06 stations (84), GR-05 breeding (89), GR-04 cultures (78), GR-02 (119), GR-03 tanks (81) and density (34; the stress 3.30 ms across both units) routes; AD-B's creature (5,000) and AD-A's slice (948) routes and the classic cycle (123), HUD (89) and card (170) routes. **The GR-11 exhibition route failed** at its second attempt: two ordinary visitors bought both Shadebacks it keeps in stock before the exhibition guest who came for one reached them, since its guest-watching stretch runs frame by frame while it tops up stock only every quarter second of the shop's clock; what visitors buy does not depend on a creature's model or size, and the same player passed the route rerun alone (60 checks, `windows-exhibition-1280x720-1-rerun`); the route is to be hardened with wave 2. Mutation checks: fourteen of eighteen breaks caught (of the gait table, the ray's beat, the sunfish's keel, the oarfish's wave and its tail, the squid's cloak, Reduced motion, sizing by span, the swim room's pulse and wave, the crown's span); four survived, each in the swim room's sampling (the crest's and keel's sway, the tail riding the wave, the gaits' own stroke sizes), each changing nothing for these eight, whose other parts reach farther.

### GR.40 — GR-13 audit and mockups (2026-10-25)

The user approved GR-12B's waves 2 to 5 ("Looks good continue"), so GR-13 started: the interface for every new system (GR.13) in the UX style. Following UX.1 ("the user approves mockups before any Unity code changes"), it starts with an audit and mockups (`Docs/Evidence/gr-13/AUDIT.md`, `Docs/Evidence/gr-13/mockups/`, published at https://claude.ai/artifact/9oRV4HdVRQrWM1AjZjqr4R).

**The audit:** the greybox already draws most of GR.13's surfaces in UI Toolkit with the UX tokens and card sheets, in a plain layout that breaks UX.3: the HUD has four fixed panels (UX.4 allows three); cards do several jobs (the incubator mixes, logs and lists its levels); explanations fill them; the journal, pinboard, catalog and research bench scroll at 1280×720; goals show as red pills; disabled reasons are button text; peeking draws two labels.

**The mockups,** each beside its greybox capture from GR-12B's run 5, drawn in the game's tokens, fonts (Baloo 2 and Nunito) and sizes at the 1920×1080 reference: the HUD (the day chip, credits with stars, one next-step line; the siphon and hands only while holding something; one note at a time; one peek tag), the incubator (Mix and Upgrade tabs, found recipes as tiles, dose chips, a meter for close to something new, the mix log moved to the journal), the shared Upgrade tab (a track of levels, the next one spelled out, gates as chips, disabled reasons on hover), containers (the occupants as a grid of spaces with schools badged, comfort as a face, Move and Sell), the breeding tank (the two parents, a timer ring, the tint port as a slot, the result as ? or the planner's prediction), the power board (a supply bar, machines as rows with draw pips and status pills, dragged to set priority), pipe placement (a mode bar), the supplier catalog (a planning view, a page per star level as tabs, item cards), the pinboard (three pinned papers: now, next era, the Grand Exhibition's rings), the journal (tabs; a shelf per family with silhouettes, hints and colour dots) and the research bench (Analyzer, Planner and Upgrade tabs).

**Claude's proposals, for the user to overrule:** stars under the credits; the goal's list off the HUD (on the pinboard, in the journal and on F1); the order of three waves (the core, then machines, then planning views).

**Approved:** the user looked at the mockups on 2026-10-25 and said "Looks good continue"; wave 1 follows.

### GR.43 — GR-14 decisions: the cut-over (2026-10-25)

The user said "Continue" after GR-13 waves 2 and 3's screenshots, so GR-13 is complete, and asked for the next task, GR-14 (GR.18: the cut-over; the new game becomes what `EvolutionShop.exe` starts). Asked first, the user chose, each as recommended:

1. **The classic game stays behind a switch.** The new game starts by default; the classic game starts with a flag (and a launcher), as the greybox did. Its routes leave the standard validation run but can still be run; removing its code may come later as a task of its own. The new game shares parts with it (the UI sheets, the player, the creature art).
2. **Cut over now, art right after.** GR-14 cuts over with the greybox rooms; a new task, **GR-16**, builds the rooms, stations, machines, pipes and sounds in the AD look (GR.14's list) in waves, each for the user's look.
3. **A short guided first night.** The welcome card and the next-step line stay; guidance in the rooms takes the player through the first loop: a marker on the next thing to use (the growers, the incubator, the tank, the counter), each step done by doing it, skippable, ending at the first sale and the night's receipt. Later systems are explained as each appears (R09.4), after GR-14. The classic Opening Night (TUT) stays with the classic game.
4. **GR-14 goes ahead with GR-07's playtest and GR-10's look still open**; the user can do them in the new default game, and their changes are folded in later.

The save policy is GR.15's and GR.17's, unchanged: a fresh start; a classic save is never overwritten or migrated, and stays for the classic game.

### GR.44 — GR-14 record: the cut-over (2026-10-25)

With the user's four choices (GR.43), the new game is the game: `EvolutionShop.exe` starts the grow-from-nothing shop, the classic game stays behind a switch, and a new game starts with a guided first night. Validated in numbered run 1 (`Docs/Evidence/gr-14-VALIDATION.md`); its gate, a full validation run, passed. How to play now: `Docs/Evidence/gr-14/PLAY.md`.

The Editor's flag is `ClassicEditorFlag`. A brand new game (no save, not a start point or a route) and Start over start the guide. The old development-only `EditorFlag` is gone.

- `Core/Growth/GrowthGuide.cs` (new): `GrowthGuideMode` (Off, On, Done, Skipped; Off for fixtures, start points and older saves), `GrowthGuideStep` (Collect, Mix, Take, Stock, Open, Sell, FirstDay), `GuideNow()` (the step read from the shop, its line, its target and the target's name), `TickGuide` (the first day with a sale over: Done, with a note), `SkipGuide`, and its save (`GrowthSave.guide`, version 11).
- `Runtime/Growth/GrowthUiGuide.cs` (new) and `GrowthUi.cs`: the next-step line says the guide's step; the marker (HudStyles' `es-arrow`, `gr-guide`) over the target, under it when there's no room above, or at the screen's edge pointing the way; hidden while a card is open. The welcome's word on the guide with **Play without guidance**, and on a classic save kept; the pause screen's **Guided first night › Skip it**; "greybox" gone from the player's words.
- Tests: EditMode `GrowthGuideTests` and `GameSwitchTests` (`Tests/RuntimeEditMode`); the GR-14 route (`GrowthGuideSmoke.cs`, `-evolutionGrowthGuideSmoke`, route key `guide`); `Docs/Evidence/gr-14/Invoke-GR14.ps1` (made from GR-13's, diffed): the launchers `Play-classic.cmd` and the start points without `-evolutionGrowth`; the guide route at both sizes; the classic routes started by their own flags.

**GR-14's choices, for the user to overrule:** the guide's seven steps and their words; it ends with the first day's receipt, not the first sale; the marker is the classic tutorial's, not a new design; `Play-greybox.cmd` is gone; the classic routes still run in this run, to show the switch works, and may be left out of later runs.

### GR.46 — GR-16: the old look set aside, three new looks to choose from (2026-10-25)

At the look board (GR.45) the user said: "I didn't really like the old design, remake it, but keep the cartoony and soft colors". So the new game's world won't take the classic game's Pocket Aquarium Diorama look (AD.1 to AD.5); it gets a new look, still cartoony and in soft colours, which the user chooses. The classic game, behind its switch, keeps its own look. The board is rewritten around three new looks (`Docs/Evidence/gr-16/board/gr16-look-board.html`, the same link: https://claude.ai/artifact/82rniL44UVq58Zpn7ZoaXD; the first board is in commit 5064729b). No game code changed.

**The three looks**, each the same corner of the shop (the window, the Starter tank with the game's own creatures, the shelf and sign, the counter, the lamp, the door to the back) and the same back room (two growers, the incubator, the pinboard, the vial shelf), from the player's eye height, rendered in Blender (EEVEE) by `Tools/Art/gr16_look_directions.py` (original, local; it appends the creatures from the DioramaKit and colours them from its manifest):
- **Soft Clay:** pastel claymation; puffy shapes with big rounded edges; butter cream, mint, peach, coral, lilac and honey; smooth shading falling into lilac; no outlines; jar growers on puffy pedestals, an egg-dome incubator.
- **Tide Pool:** a house in a tide pool; seafoam walls with a scalloped band, portholes and arches, a sand floor with pebbles, coral pink trim; shading into sea blue; bathysphere growers, a diving-helmet incubator, a coral rock under the tank, a ribbed shell counter, a jellyfish lamp; its machines to come would be diving and submarine gear.
- **Storybook:** a picture book; cream and sage, dusty blue and rose; a soft ink outline on every object, two-tone shading, shapes a little hand-cut; painted floor boards, a curtained window, a paper lantern, mason-jar growers, a bell-jar incubator; the outlines cost the most to run.

**Claude's suggestion on the board, for the user to overrule:** Tide Pool's shapes with Soft Clay's puffier edges and warmer colours. **Next:** the user's choice (one, or a mix); then the chosen look worked up (the two rooms by day and by night, and sketches of the stations not shown yet), for the user's look again; then wave 1 in game. The five waves (GR.45) stand, toward the new look. What the classic look's code can still lend is its machinery (converting a scene's materials to a toon style, a Volume per room, light keys, the flipbook moments, the comfort rules), not its models or palette.

### GR.47 — GR-16: one new look, in the feel the user asked for (2026-10-25)

The user turned down the three looks of GR.46 too: asked whether they differed in colours or in 3D models as well (both), they said "So it's both sucks", and chose, when asked, that the shapes were too basic, the colours too pale and flat, and none was the style they wanted; the reference for the feel: **Slime Rancher** (bouncy, saturated pastels, glossy, sunny; nothing copied). So the board now leads with one new look built that way (same link: https://claude.ai/artifact/82rniL44UVq58Zpn7ZoaXD; `Docs/Evidence/gr-16/board/`), the three first looks below it as turned down. No game code changed.

**The look** (`Tools/Art/gr16_look_ranch.py`, Blender 5.0 EEVEE with ray tracing, AgX Punchy, a soft bloom; about 40 s for its three renders): juicy soft colours (turquoise `#22BFB0` and sunny yellow `#FFC93C` as the shop's own, coral, fresh green, warm wood with its grain, cream; Bloom `#FF5E7E` and Flow `#3FA9FF` for the cultures); glossy plastic with a clear coat; cartoon glass that is clear face-on with a pale edge; sunlight through a real window opening onto a 3D landscape (hills, round trees, a fence, puffy clouds); warm pendant lamps; cultures and screens that glow. **Stations with their parts:** a grower (three feet, a base with turquoise bands, a gauge with a needle, a glass tube of glowing culture with bubbles, a domed cap with a light, a pipe of straight runs with round joints); the incubator (a yellow base with a panel of lights, a glowing ring, a nest with the two doses under a glass dome, a console on a stalk showing the mix as coloured bars, a big button); the Starter tank (a cream cabinet with turquoise doors, a frame, gravel, plants, a rock, a light bar, the game's creatures); the counter (a plank front, a cream top, a register with a screen and keys, a bell, a treat jar, a plant). **Rooms:** the shop (plank floor, cream walls over turquoise board panelling, chunky beams, the sign, a shelf of jars, a poster, the door with its round window, crates, a clock, a rug); the back room (mint walls with turquoise panels, a cream and turquoise checker floor, the growers piped up the wall and over to the incubator, a pinboard, a vial rack, a bench of flasks). Three renders: the shop and the back room from the player's eye height, and the stations side by side.

**Found while building:** one room's landscape hill was large enough to bury the other room, which hid the sun (the rooms are now far apart); the sun was too weak beside the rooms' lamps to show its patch; and fully clear glass made the growers' tubes and the incubator's dome vanish.

**Next:** the user's word on the direction. If yes, the same rooms at night and the stations not shown yet in this style, then wave 1 in game; or the changes they ask for.

### GR.48 — GR-16: the new look approved; the rooms at night and every machine in it (2026-10-25)

**The user approved the new look (GR.47) on 2026-10-25: "So much better this is great".** It is GR-16's look from here: juicy soft colours (turquoise and sunny yellow, coral, fresh green, warm wood, cream; Bloom, Flow and Mist for the cultures), glossy plastic, cartoon glass, sunlight through real windows, warm lamps, glowing cultures and screens, and stations built from their parts; Slime Rancher as the feel only.

**Drawn since, in that look** (`Tools/Art/gr16_look_ranch.py`, on the board, same link; `Docs/Evidence/gr-16/board/img/ranch-*.jpg`):
- **The two rooms at night** (`--night`): moonlight through the windows, a dark blue sky with the moon and stars, fireflies outside; the pendant lamps carry the shop, and the back room is lit mostly by its glowing cultures, the tank and the incubator's ring (cultures and water glow twice as much at night).
- **Every station and machine the game builds** (from the greybox's own list, `Runtime/Growth/GrowthWorld*.cs`), each at its top level so its levels' parts show: the back room's grower with its hand pump (a yellow crank wheel on its side), the conditioning vat (a glass column of lilac mist with its second batch and a stirrer motor), the breeding tank (a cream cabinet with a heart, a glass divider making the nursery, a funnel tint port, a pair and a hatchling), the research bench (microscope, the analyzer's glowing screen, the siphon's twin bulb and canister, the planner board) and the supplier catalog (an open book on a tilted lectern); the annex's and workshop's generator at level 1 (a hand crank with a coral wheel) and level 4 (the tide engine: three glass columns of bubbles over a ribbed drum), buffers (numbered glass columns of culture), the power board (supply and draw bars, a lamp) and the sorter (a slatted belt carrying a hatchling, a glass hopper fed by the chute, honey and coral gates over their trays).

**Next:** wave 1 in game (GR.45's slice), unless the user asks for a machine to change: the back room and shop unit A in the new look (the rooms, growers, incubator, Starter tank and counter, the day and night light), modelled in Blender as kit pieces and placed as decorative children of the greybox's objects (AD.4's rule: positions, colliders, targets and save ids unchanged), validated in a numbered run with captures by day and night and the densest layout's timings, then the user's look in game.

### GR.49 — GR-16 wave 1: the new look in game (2026-10-25)

The user said "Continue" after the night renders and the machine sheets (GR.48), so wave 1 (GR.45's slice) puts the approved look in the game: the back room and shop unit A, where every shop starts, and their stations.
