**Built:**
- `Tools/Art/create_growth_kit.py` (new; Blender 5.0, original, local, about 11 s): the growth kit, 21 FBX in `Resources/Art/GrowthKit/` with `ArtSource/GrowthKit/manifest.json`. Each piece is one joined mesh directly under its root (the diorama kit's probed mapping, Unity (x, y, z) to Blender (-x, -z, y)), its material slots named GK_*; the stations' pieces at their greybox root and size (the grower and its ring, taps and lid; the incubator and its screen, tint port and tray, auto-doser port; the Starter tank's cabinet and frame, its bed of pebbles and plants, filter and lid; the counter and its bag dispenser, card reader and second till), and the rooms' dressing at the world's origin (unit A's planks, panelling, rails, beams, frames, lamps, window, sign, shelf, poster, clock, plants and rug; the street outside; the back room's tiles, panels, mint wall, frames, light strip and plant).
- `Runtime/Growth/GrowthWorldLook.cs` (new): `LookOn` (off with `-evolutionGrowthPlain`); `LookSlots`, the GK_* palette (plastic with its smoothness, glowing, metal, the classic look's rim-lit glass, the culture's own); `RestyleForLook` gives the greybox's own materials the look's palette before anything is built (every room, dressed or not); `Kit` places a piece as a decorative child, maps its slots and switches off only the replaced greybox parts' renderers (their colliders and targets stay); `DressRooms`, `DressStations`, `DressSource`, `DressTank` (Starter tanks; the harmony frame sits on the kit's rim), `DressLevel` (from GR-06's `Look`); the light: four keys (night, warm and lamplit since night is when the player works; morning, midday and closing by the day's clock) blended over 0.6 s, sun, trilight ambient, lamps, the camera's sky colour, and a global Volume (bloom, Neutral tonemapping, colour, white balance, vignette: only effects the project's profiles already use); a realtime reflection probe in each dressed room, rendered once the room stands and again when night and day change over (realtime probes are switched on at run time; the project keeps them off for the classic game).
- `GrowthWorld.cs`: the hooks (restyle, dress, level parts, the tinted dome and breeding water in the look's glass, the light each frame). `Editor/RedesignModelImporter.cs`: the GrowthKit folder imports as the other kits do.
- Tests: EditMode `GrowthLookTests` (every piece loads as one mesh whose slots the look maps and brings no collider; the manifest lists every piece and the palette's colours; each station's piece within its footprint; each room's dressing inside its room); the GR-16 look route (`GrowthLookSmoke.cs`, `-evolutionGrowthLookSmoke`, route key `look`: the dressing and pieces present, the greybox's shapes hidden but solid, the reticle still focusing each station, the look's materials, the grade, the reflections, every level's pieces, the four light keys, the frame budget in both rooms, and captures by night and day); `Docs/Evidence/gr-16/Invoke-GR16.ps1` (made from GR-14's and diffed: the look route at both sizes, and the density route again with `-evolutionGrowthPlain`, to measure what the look costs).

**GR-16's choices, for the user to overrule:** night warm and lamplit rather than dark; the sign over unit A's back wall, the shelf of jars, the poster, the clock and the plants where they stand; the Starter tank's light bar, pebbles and plants, the counter's treats and plant, and the growers' gauges as decoration; the vat, the breeding tank, the research bench, Large and Show tanks, racks and the other rooms in the look's colours until their waves.

**Found while building:** the back room's wall panels ran past the walls' ends (the kit's room test caught it; whole panels, centred, now); `Transform.Find` read the slash in a "Look / rooms" holder as a path; the back room's side of the wall it shares with unit A wore the shop's plaster; realtime reflection probes are off in the project's quality settings; night at first was dark and cold (the undressed rooms gloomy); the grower's taps floated behind its jar; the clouds were grey; indoors, day and night read alike until the sun was stronger and the day's ambient softer.

**Validation (run 1):** every step passed, with the Windows development player `Builds/Windows-6000.3.21f1-gr16-look-1` (245.6 MB, 0 errors, 0 warnings): EditMode 2,607 of 2,607 (with `GrowthLookTests`, 9); the GR-16 look route, 74 checks at 1280×720 and 1920×1080, both dressed rooms inside the frame budget; the guide (65), GR-02 (127), tanks (85) and breeding (89) routes at both sizes; the density route with the look and without it (34 each); cultures 78, stations 84, power 91, line 77, helpers 98, spaces 60, exhibition 60, creatures 122; the classic cycle 123, HUD 89 and cards 170 behind their switch. What the look costs, uncapped on the density route: nothing measurable (unit A with 120 creatures 2.96 / 4.86 ms average and 95th percentile with it, 3.27 / 6.14 without; every view within the run-to-run spread both ways). Three deliberate breaks of `GrowthWorldLook.cs` (a slot left unmapped, a palette colour off by a digit, the kit's folder misnamed), each run against `GrowthLookTests`: all three caught. The board (same link) now shows the game's captures beside the sketches. Tag `gr16-wave1-validated`.

- `Core/Growth/GrowthStarts.cs`: the look start gains unit C and the hall, Show tanks at levels 2 and 1 on c1 and c2 and a Large tank at level 3 on e1, with creatures, bought with the shop's own actions.

**Built (wave 5):**
- `Runtime/Growth/GrowthAudio.cs` (new): six sounds made at start, original (`MakeHum`, `MakeMotor`, `MakeClick`, `MakeStroke`, `MakePop`, `MakeSwish`): the hand crank's ratchet every 0.12 s while cranking, the hand pump's squelch and glug every 0.45 s while pumping, the generator's hum while a turbine makes power (pitch 1.0, 0.9 and 0.8 for levels 2 to 4), the electric pumps' whirr from the running pump nearest the player, the breeding pop when an offspring arrives, a school's swish. Five 3D voices placed where each happens (linear falloff from 2 to 16 m); loops are whole cycles in four seconds and one-shots start and end at silence. Nothing new plays while paused and the loops fade out. `GrowthMoments` notices the moments by comparing the shop with how it was (a shop just loaded, or a tank seen for the first time, marks nothing).
- Settings: the pause screen's **Sound** row with two chips, **Sounds** and **Hum**, each Full, Half or Off (`GrowthAudio.Level`), kept with the slot (`GrowthSave.soundLevel`, `humLevel`; an older save without them is Full). One row, and the guided first night's Skip it a line under the settings rather than a row of its own, so the pause card still fits at 1280×720 without scrolling (two rows of their own made it scroll).

**Fixed:** the slot's comfort settings (Peek, Reduced motion, the mouse) were saved but never read back, so a shop opened with them at their defaults; `GrowthSession` now reads them back with the two new ones (`ReadSettings`, `ApplySettings`). In the look, the annex's and workshop's lamps faded up to the greybox's 1.15 and then jumped to the light key's at the end of their reveal; every room's lamps now fade up to the key's. The GR-10 spaces route's lamp checks compared with the greybox's 1.25 and now read the room's full intensity from the world (`LampFull`).

**Tests:** `GrowthLookTests` (now 39): wave 4's pieces in the manifest, each one mesh with the look's slots; `WaveFoursPiecesStayWhereTheyBelong` (the bench in front of its wall and below head height but its bowls, the feature tank between the feature places and against the wall, the feature wall a paint layer, each board, the sign and the award on its wall, each partition within its doorway and as thick as the greybox's, the banners within their cloth, a fixture under the ceiling, the display on its sill); units B and C inside their rooms; the arch into unit C added to `NoRoomsDressingCrossesADoorway`, with units B's and C's dressing and the feature wall; the look start's wave 4. `GrowthAudioTests` (new, 7): each sound made in the game and never clipping, one-shots starting and ending at silence and over before the next, loops four seconds and seamless, the settings' gains and cycle, the settings kept with the save (an older save Full and still loading), a school and an offspring each noticed once. The look route (now 309 checks) gains wave 4 (every piece, the hidden greybox parts still solid, the lamps at the key, the reticle on the boards and the award, the exhibition's pieces in each state, the partitions closed on two fixtures, unit C opened in the look) and wave 5 (the hum and whirr where they happen, at half and off, out while paused, the ratchet and strokes held with the route's mouse, a pop and a swish, a swish at zero with Sounds off), captures 460 to 475 and 480, and unit B's and unit C's timings (425, 426).

**GR-16's choices, for the user to overrule:** listed in LOOK.md's last section.

**Found while building:** the feature wall's wavy edge ran 40 cm past its end, behind the hall's plans (the Blender export's bounds showed it, fixed before it reached the game); wave 4's lettering took up to 50,000 triangles a piece (`text()`'s coarser resolution); two rows of their own for the sounds made the pause screen scroll at 1280×720, so they share one and the guided first night's Skip it is a line; the sound test caught the swish peaking at 0.84 (it would have clipped); and three mistakes in the route itself on its first scratch run (the exhibition held without its results record, the hum at half measured while still fading up, a name reused), fixed before the numbered run.

**Validation (run 5):** `Docs/Evidence/gr-16/Invoke-GR16.ps1 -Run 5` on 2026-10-26, the player `Builds/Windows-6000.3.21f1-gr16-look-5` (263.3 MB, no errors or warnings), **every step passing, first time**: 2,644 EditMode tests (`GrowthLookTests` 39, `GrowthAudioTests` 7); the GR-16 look route, 309 checks at 1280×720 and 1920×1080, every room, units B and C included, at the display's refresh (8.34 ms, vSync on), the worst frame 41.1 ms once (the workshop at 1280×720), otherwise at most 12.5 ms; the guide, GR-02, tanks and breeding routes at both sizes (65, 127, 85, 89); cultures 78, stations 84, power 91, line 77, helpers 98, spaces 60, exhibition 60, creatures 122; the density route with the look and without it (34 each); the classic cycle, HUD and card routes (123, 89, 170). What the look costs on the densest layouts: at most about a quarter of a millisecond on average (a day of visitors, 1.96 against 1.72 ms), elsewhere within the run-to-run spread both ways, against 16.7 ms on average and 25 ms at the 95th percentile. **Deliberate breaks:** six, each alone against `GrowthLookTests` and `GrowthAudioTests` (all 46 passing unbroken first), then reverted, **all caught**: the feature wall's wavy edge past its end again and the bay window's bowls at head height (each rebuilt in Blender; `WaveFoursPiecesStayWhereTheyBelong`), a shop just loaded popping for the offspring already waiting (`AnOffspringArrivingIsNoticedOnce`), the one-shots without their fades (`AOneShotStartsAndEndsAtSilence` and `EachSoundIsMadeHereAndNeverClips`), an older save's sounds at half (`TheSettingsAreKeptWithTheSaveAndAnOlderSaveIsFull`), and the look start without unit C and the hall (`TheLookStartHasEveryDressedStationAndTankAtItsTopLevel`). The sixth first failed to apply, its anchor matching two start points, and was run again alone with a unique one. The board has waves 4 and 5's captures (460 to 472, and 480). Tags `gr16-wave4-validated` and `gr16-wave5-validated`.

### GR.53 — The user's decisions at GR-16's end: the old game removed, characters next, the playthrough last (2026-10-28)

After playing waves 4 and 5 (GR.52), the user answered the next steps put to them (`"ok so i did 1 its good, you can close 2 its doesn't matter anymore, and i think you forgot about character design and animation, 3 can move to the back only when we implemented all we want from the game, 4 will be after character, anf i think you can remove all that was related to the older game"`):

1. **GR-16 is complete:** the user approved waves 4 and 5 (units B and C, the hall, the boards and signs; the sounds) in game.
2. **The open gates are closed:** GR-07's playtest, GR-10's look, GR-09's second-till rule (it stays as built: the front of the queue is the player's while they hold the counter), AD-D's look and AD-E's playtest.
3. **Characters next (GR-18):** the new game's people and the player's hands, designed in the approved look and animated. Today the visitors and helpers are greybox figures (a capsule body, a sphere head and nose, `GrowthVisitorView` and `GrowthWorldHelpers`), and the player has no hands. A look board first, then waves in game, each for the user's look.
4. **The building work of R09 and R10 after the characters:** R09.4 (onboarding in the later rooms), R10.3 (the settings still missing), R10.4 and R10.5 (the densest layout, saves, windows and screens).
5. **GR-15 to the back:** the full playthrough and tuning comes once everything the user wants from the game is in, with R09.5, R10.1 and R10.2 folded into it; R10.6 and R11 (the release) after it.
6. **The old game is removed (GR-17):** asked how far, the user chose **the board and the code** (the classic game deleted from the project, keeping only what the new game uses; its tasks taken off the board into an archive with their history; a tag on the last commit with it; a full validation run of the new game) and **deleting its builds and evidence from disk**, told it cannot be undone.

### GR.54 — GR-17: the old game removed (2026-10-28)

**GR-17 complete — 2026-10-28.** After the user approved GR-16's last waves in game, they asked to "remove all that was related to the older game" (GR.53) and, asked how far, chose **the board and the code**, and **deleting the old builds and evidence from disk**, told it cannot be undone. The classic game (the shop the project was until GR-14's cut-over, behind `-evolutionClassic` since) is gone from the project; the game is the only game. Its last commit is tagged `old-game-last` (`7a35cd21`).

#### What changed

- **Code:** 345 of the project's 428 C# files deleted (about 77,000 lines): the classic rules (23 in `Core`), sessions, routes and probes (157 in `Runtime`, `Runtime/UI` included), presentation and effects (65 in `Presentation`), Editor tools (2: the FX hands importer, Checkpoint 00) and 98 test files. 83 remain.
- **What the new game still used, kept and cut down:** `ShopSession` (now only the scene's start, handing over to `GrowthSession`, and the command line's `Arg`); `ShopPlayer`; `ShopWorld` (now only `Shape` and `MakeMaterial`); `ShopUiRoot` (now only the UI's resource names and panel scale); `HudElements` (now only the guide's arrow); `ShopUiTheme` and `ShopUiTestDriver` as they were; the toon look (`DioramaLook`, without the classic light keys, hatch moment, look switch and street window shader) and the creature kit and its rig (`DioramaCreatures` without the classic tanks' water, `CreatureVisual` without the classic procedural placeholder creatures, `CreatureVisualDiorama`, `CreatureMotion`, `PondletMotion`, `FxHatchChoreography`).
- **The switch:** `-evolutionClassic`, the Editor's **Play the classic game** menu and `GrowthGreybox.ClassicAsked` and `Requested` gone; the guide route's five switch checks with them. The welcome no longer says the classic game's save is kept; the save itself (`evolution-shop.json`) is left on the player's computer as it was, never read, and the routes check the game never writes it.
- **Assets:** the classic kits (`RedesignKit`, `LabRefreshKit`, `CollectorKit`, the FX hands), AD-C's seven characters and AD-D's effect flipbook from the creature kit, the classic shaders (effect particles, world text, street window), the classic UI's layouts and strings, Checkpoint 00's material and the classic save fixtures. The creature kit's Blender script, manifest and `.blend` lost the characters and flipbook too (the 72 creatures and the atlas as last built, not rebuilt); the other kits' scripts and sources deleted.
- **Records:** the old game's statuses, milestones (R01–R08, TUT, FX, UX, AD), handoffs, delivery records and specifications moved from `AGENTS.md` to `Docs/Archive/old-game-AGENTS.md` (`AGENTS.md` from 5,879 lines to about 1,660); the original brief and the old planning documents to `Docs/Archive/`; the README rewritten for the game.
- **Deleted from disk:** the old game's evidence (52,396 tracked files in 1,715 entries, and 27 untracked folders) and its 173 builds (about 30 GB); the 31 GR builds and every GR evidence folder kept.

#### Validation (numbered run 2)

Run with `gr-17/Invoke-GR17.ps1 -Run 2` on 2026-10-28, the Windows development player `Builds/Windows-6000.3.21f1-gr17-one-game-2` (no classic routes any more). **Every step passed.** Run 1 (the same steps on candidate `4346389d`) passed every step but the look route at 1280×720, which stopped aiming at the hand crank: it had set the generator back to level 1 and aimed two frames later, but the project does not sync physics with every transform change, so the crank, just rebuilt and moved back to the annex, was not yet there for the ray, which met the wall behind it (it passed at 1920×1080 and in GR-16's runs by the timing). The route now waits for the crank's look and a physics step before aiming (`9049ff6e`); run 2 is on that.

| Step | Result |
|---|---|
| EditMode, the full suite (`gr-17/editmode-all-2`) | 194 of 194 (the classic game's tests went with it: 2,644 at GR-16) |
| Windows development build (`gr-17/windows-build-2`) | succeeded, 0 errors, 0 warnings, 238.7 MB (263.3 MB at GR-16) |
| GR-16 look route, 1280×720 and 1920×1080 | 309 checks each |
| GR-14 guide route, 1280×720 and 1920×1080 | 60 checks each (65 at GR-16: its five checks of the removed switch went with it) |
| GR-02 route, 1280×720 and 1920×1080 | 127 checks each |
| GR-03 tanks route, 1280×720 and 1920×1080 | 85 checks each |
| GR-05 breeding route, 1280×720 and 1920×1080 | 89 checks each |
| GR-03 density route, with the look and without it | 34 checks each; what the look costs: below |
| GR-04 cultures, GR-06 stations, GR-07 power, GR-08 line, GR-09 helpers | 78, 84, 91, 77 and 98 checks |
| GR-10 spaces, GR-11 exhibition, GR-12 creatures | 60, 60 and 122 checks |

**What the look costs** (the density route, uncapped, average and 95th percentile in ms):

| View | With the look | Without it |
|---|---|---|
| 120 creatures in unit A | 3.50 / 5.39 | 3.30 / 6.26 |
| unit B | 2.51 / 3.82 | 2.58 / 4.18 |
| across both units | 2.57 / 3.77 | 2.68 / 4.33 |
| the Show tank, close | 3.06 / 4.83 | 3.30 / 5.86 |
| the back room | 2.57 / 4.14 | 2.40 / 4.48 |
| 180 creatures in unit A | 4.27 / 6.37 | 4.50 / 8.48 |
| 180 across | 3.36 / 5.13 | 3.30 / 5.92 |

At most about a quarter of a millisecond on average (a day of visitors, 1.97 against 1.72 ms), elsewhere within the run-to-run spread, against a budget of 16.7 ms on average and 25 ms at the 95th percentile: as at GR-16.

**Not done:** no deliberate breaks: GR-17 adds no rule of its own to break, and every rule the new game has kept its tests and routes, which ran as they did at GR-16. The checks of the removed switch went with it (the guide route's five), and the EditMode suite shrank from 2,644 tests to 194 with the classic game's.

### GR.55 — GR-18 audit and look board (2026-10-29)

The user said "Yes, Start with gr-18" after GR-17, so GR-18, the characters' design and animation they asked for at GR-16's end (GR.53), started. As GR-16 did, it starts with an audit and a look board for the user (`Docs/Evidence/gr-18/AUDIT.md`, `Docs/Evidence/gr-18/board/gr18-look-board.html`, published at https://claude.ai/artifact/Jmur3DCfHT2FCMJjSnmsg2). No game code changed.

**The audit:** the visitors are capsules in their kind's colour with a sphere head and nose (`GrowthVisitorView`), gliding along the routes with no limb moving; the helpers are the same capsule with an apron and cap, a hand point holding what they carry (`GrowthWorldHelpers.cs`); the player has a camera and no hands, though they siphon, serve, crank, carry up to three creatures, put them in and take them out, and lay pipes. At most five ordinary visitors are in at once, six guests on the exhibition's day, and three helpers. None of §6.7's comedy is in the new game; it went with the classic game at GR-17.

**The look on the board** (`Tools/Art/gr18_characters.py`, original and local, built on GR-16's look script): glossy cartoon figures of rounded parts, three heads tall, in the world's own colours and plastic, with the creatures' kind of eyes. Each kind of visitor keeps its greybox colour and gets a mark: the casual visitor a hoodie and a tote; the bargain hunter a bucket hat, round glasses, a cardigan, a belt bag, coupons and a net bag; the collector a sweater vest, a bow tie, a loupe, a satchel and a notebook; the big spender a long coat with gold buttons, sunglasses, a gold chain and shopping bags; the guests a long gold coat, a sash and rosette, a top hat and a programme. Every visitor is drawn from their kind by a seed (skin tone, hair style and colour, height, build, features, the outfit's colours, sometimes a hat or glasses). The helpers wear the shop's turquoise: Pip a pink polo and cap with the shop's apron and a name badge; Nori a green coverall with the shop's mark, a bandana and yellow boots, carrying a jar; Moss a lab coat, goggles over green curls, lilac gloves, a pipette and a clipboard. The player's hands: yellow work gloves and turquoise sleeves rolled up in cream; creatures carried in the shop's water bags, which visitors take home too. Rendered: the cast on two sheets, a face for each, seven casual visitors' variety, three builds (2.6, 3 and 3.8 heads tall), the shop from behind the counter (the player's hand holding out a bag) and at the Starter tank, the back room with Nori and Moss, the player carrying three bags and siphoning; and three loops (the visitors' walks, what visitors do, the helpers at work).

**How they would be built and moved:** a Blender kit with one mesh per joint and parts for each hair style, hat, outfit and held item, assembled at runtime from a visitor's seed; motions as keyframes on the joints from the same script, saved as data the game samples (no Animator), blended, with the walk's step matched to the distance moved; the root, colliders, targets, routes, queue spots and save ids unchanged. Three waves: (1) the visitors and guests, (2) the helpers at work, (3) the player's hands; each ending in a numbered run, captures and the densest layout's timings, and the user's look in game.

**Found while drawing:** held items made at rest turned with the arm into odd places (they are now made after the pose, where the hand is); flat boxes for aprons read as boards (now panels curved on the torso's oval); long coats read as dresses until their tails opened at the front; first-person arms came in from the sides until they rose from the bottom of the view; one shot's objects showed in another's renders until each shot had a collection of its own.

**The user's answers (2026-10-29):** "Looks good": the look approved as drawn; the build as drawn, 3 heads tall; the player's hands in the shop's yellow gloves; and §6.7's comedy back as a fourth wave of GR-18 once the people can react (the slap, the suspicious fart, the ridiculous complaint, the incubator's belch, the overenthusiastic helper, the fish copying a customer, with §6.7's limits). So GR-18 has four waves: (1) visitors and guests, (2) helpers, (3) the player's hands, (4) the comedy.

### GR.56 — GR-18 wave 1: the visitors and guests as people (2026-10-29)

After the user approved the look board (GR.55), wave 1 puts the visitors of every kind and the exhibition's guests in the game as people of that look, where the greybox had coloured capsules. Validation: `Docs/Evidence/gr-18-WAVE1-VALIDATION.md`; for the user: `Docs/Evidence/gr-18/LOOK.md`.

**How they are made:** a people kit (`Tools/Art/create_people_kit.py`, original and local, Blender 5.0) built with the look board's own builder, so the game's people are the board's: a body for each kind of visitor (one mesh per joint and variant, four hand shapes a side, the casual visitor's trousers or shorts), the head's parts (skull, eyes, lids, lashes, brows, three noses, five mouths, a mustache, freckles, a beauty mark, every hair style, fringe and hat, glasses and sunglasses) and what visitors hold (the shop's water bag, coins, coupons, a net bag, a notebook, shopping bags, a programme), with `people.json` for the game; materials are named slots the game fills with each person's colours. The game (`GrowthPeople.cs`) assembles a person on a chain of joints, scaled by height, build and head size, with a look of their own from the visitor's id (skin, hair and its colour, height, build, face, nose, smile, the outfit's colours, sometimes a hat or glasses, the same in every run), and moves them with the board's motions ported as code: each kind's walk with its step matched to the distance walked, browsing (facing the tank, leaning in, pointing), paying (the coins held out), waiting (a tapping foot and a look at the time as patience runs low, the mouth falling open), and leaving with what they bought in the shop's bag and a grin, with a shrug, in a huff, or with a wave at closing. Where they go stays the rules' and the routes'; `-evolutionGrowthPlain` keeps the capsules.

**Found while building:** the coins showed in a relaxed hand at the counter (a later line set the hand back every frame); a visitor at a side tank faced the way they walked in, along the wall; a person's look hung on `string.GetHashCode`, which is not promised to be the same between runs; and the collector's square glasses, solid plates on the board, hid the eyes, so they have a rim round a clear lens now (the one change to the approved look, for the user to overrule).

**Validation (run 1):** EditMode 200 of 200 (GR-18's six people tests among them); development player `gr18-people-1` (256.6 MB, 238.7 at GR-17); the GR-18 people route (104 checks) at both sizes; the GR-16 look (309), GR-14 guide (60), GR-02 (127), GR-03 tanks (85) and GR-05 breeding (89) routes at both sizes; the GR-12 creatures (122), GR-11 exhibition (60), GR-10 spaces (60), GR-09 helpers (98), GR-08 line (77), GR-07 power (91), GR-06 stations (84), GR-04 cultures (78) and GR-03 density (34, with and without the look) routes. Eleven people in unit A by day: 1.89 ms a frame uncapped (2.57 at the 95th percentile); a day of visitors 2.08 ms against 1.97 with GR-17's capsules.

**Deliberate breaks** (each against `GrowthPeopleTests`, then put back): a joint's turn dropping its roll (caught: the board's angles), the walk sinking the body (caught: the floor), every visitor of a kind the same whatever their seed (caught: no two alike), and the head's parts left off but the skull (survived: the test checked the look, not the parts on the head; it now checks every head part the look asks for is on the person, and caught the break; the six tests pass with nothing broken). The floor test catches sinking; floating is checked only standing, in the route.

**The user's gate:** their look in game (LOOK.md), then wave 2, the helpers.

### GR.57 — GR-18 wave 2: the helpers as people at work (2026-10-29)

After wave 1 the user said "Continue", so wave 2 followed: Pip, Nori and Moss as people of the approved look at work, where the greybox had a capsule with an apron and a cap. Validation: `Docs/Evidence/gr-18-WAVE2-VALIDATION.md`; for the user: `Docs/Evidence/gr-18/LOOK.md` (waves 1 and 2).

**How they are made:** the people kit gained the board's cast (bodies in their own colours, Moss in lilac gloves) and the board's jar, pipette (its fill a role the game colours) and clipboard; their faces are the head kit's (Pip's pigtails, cap and freckles; Nori's crop, bandana and bushy brows; Moss's curls and goggles). Each helper is a person with a fixed look who follows the rules' step as the capsule did: walking it in the board's gait (Pip bouncy, Nori carrying the jar out in front, Moss leaning forward), at the step's pick-up or put-down moment doing the board's work (Pip's scanner sweep, nod and wave at the till; Nori's reach over the rim; Moss's three drops and a look at the clipboard), and otherwise standing at their post. The creatures Nori carries swim in the jar; Moss carries a parent in the jar too; a dose fills Moss's pipette in its culture's colour, rinsed pale once it is in.

**Found while building:** the jar lay on its side in Nori's hands. The kit made held items at rest, so whatever is held up turned with the wrist; wave 1's notebook, programme, coins and bag were tipped too. The kit now makes each item in the arm pose that holds it and puts the arm back to rest, and a test checks they stand upright there.

**Validation (run 2):** EditMode 204 of 204; development player `gr18-people-2` (247.1 MB); the people route (154 checks, the helpers' 50) at both sizes; the GR-09 helpers route (98, the helpers' steps now drawn as people); the GR-16 look (309), GR-14 guide (60), GR-02 (127), GR-03 tanks (85) and GR-05 breeding (89) routes at both sizes; the GR-12 creatures (122), GR-11 exhibition (60), GR-10 spaces (60), GR-08 line (77), GR-07 power (91), GR-06 stations (84), GR-04 cultures (78) and GR-03 density (34, with and without the look) routes. The helpers: 34,287 to 51,359 triangles each. Eleven people in unit A by day 1.88 ms a frame uncapped; a day of visitors 2.07 ms (2.08 at wave 1).

**Deliberate breaks** (each against `GrowthPeopleTests`, then put back): the pipette never taking the dose's colour was caught (the pipette's colour); Nori without the jar was caught (the cast, and the items upright); Nori's walk without the carry was caught (the helpers at work and walking: the arms steady under the jar); Pip's till without the wave was caught (the same test: the arm going up); the jar's holding arms taken away **survived**: the upright test checked the jar's bounds, which did not pin the arms. It now checks that the arms holding the jar, the pipette and the clipboard come up to them, and caught the break; the ten tests pass with nothing broken (run 2 ran before that check was added).

**The user's gate:** their look in game at waves 1 and 2 (LOOK.md), then wave 3, the player's hands.
